L505: ## 6 Clinical Pharmacology
L506: ## 6.1. Executive Summary
L507: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L508: The proposed indication is for use of quizartinib (VANFLYTA®) in combination with standard cytarabine and anthracycline induction and standard cytarabine consolidation chemotherapy, and as continuation monotherapy following consolidation, for the treatment of adult patients with newly diagnosed acute myeloid leukemia (AML) that is FMS-like tyrosine kinase 3 internal tandem duplication (FLT3-ITD) positive as detected by an FDA-approved test.
L509: The marketed formulation of quizartinib is as the salt in tablets; however, doses in the label are described as free base content. Quizartinib is available as 20 mg and 30 mg (salt form) tablets. In the assessment aid, doses are generally described as free base; however, there are instances where the applicant has described the dose in the salt form. **Table 9** provides the translation between the dose descriptions. Quizartinib dosing regimen is proposed as described in **Table 10**.
L510: **Table 9: Translation between free base and salt form of quizartinib**
L511: Free base content	Salt form
17.7 mg	20 mg
26.5 mg	30 mg
35.4 mg	40 mg
53 mg	60 mg

L515: The evidence of efficacy for the proposed indication comes from the pivotal study AC220-A-U302: A Phase 3, Randomized (1:1), Double-Blind, Placebo-Controlled Study: Quizartinib vs Placebo with Standard Chemotherapy in FLT3-ITD(+) ND AML (QuANTUM-First) demonstrating median (95% CI) OS was 31.9 (21.0, not estimable (NE)) months in the quizartinib arm compared to 15.1 (13.2, 26.2) months in the standard chemotherapy arm.
L516: In the safety cohort of 265 patients in the quizartinib arm, quizartinib at the proposed dosage resulted in a Grade \ge 3 TEAE rate of 47.5%, and a serious TEAE rate of 54.0%. Dose reduction due to an AE occurred in 18.9% of patients. Dose interruption occurred in 34.0% of patients and dose discontinuation due to AE occurred in 20.4% of patients.
L517: Quizartinib prolongs the QT interval in a concentration-related manner. In study AC220-A-U302, 34.3% of patients on the quizartinib arm had new occurrence of a QTcF &gt;450 msec, compared to 17.9% in the placebo arm. In 7.5% of these patients, the QTcF &gt;480 msec, and in 2.3% of patients it &gt;500 msec. A &gt;30 msec increase from baseline was observed in 55.1% of patients. The increase from baseline was &gt;60 msec in 10.2% of patients.
L518: The clinical pharmacology review focused on the evaluation of the proposed dosage, drug-drug interactions, QT interval prolongation, the impact of organ impairment, and the impact of FLT3-ITD VAF and *NPM1* mutation status. Population PK (PPK), exposure-response (E-R), and physiologically-based PK (PBPK) modeling analyses were also conducted and reviewed.
L519: ## Recommendations
L520: The Office of Clinical Pharmacology has reviewed the information and data contained in NDA 216993. This NDA is approvable from a Clinical Pharmacology perspective with the PMR discussed in Section 6.2.2.3 and included in Section 13.
L521: Review Issue	Recommendations/Comments
Pivotal or supportive evidence of effectiveness	The evidence of efficacy for the proposed indication comes from the pivotal study AC220-A-U302: A Phase 3, Randomized (1:1), Double-Blind, Placebo-Controlled Study: Quizartinib vs Placebo with Standard Chemotherapy in FLT3-ITD(+) ND AML (QuANTUM-First) demonstrating median (95% CI) OS was 31.9 (21.0, not estimable (NE)] months) in the quizartinib arm compared to 15.1 (13.2, 26.2) months in the placebo arm.
General dosing instructions (free base)	Induction: (up to 2 cycles) Starting on Day 8 (for 7 + 3 regimen) 35.4 mg once daily from Day 8 to Day 21.
Consolidation: (up to 4 cycles) Starting on Day 6, 35.4 mg once daily from Day 6 to Day 19.
Maintenance: Starting dose: 26.5 mg once daily for two weeks if QTcF \le450 ms, then increase the dose to 53 mg once daily if QTcF \le450 ms.
Dosing in patient subgroups (intrinsic and extrinsic factors)	The following dose modifications are recommended:
Strong and moderate CYP3A4 inducers: Avoid coadministration with strong or moderate CYP3A4 inducers.
Strong CYP3A4 inhibitors: Reduce the dose of VANFLYTA if coadministered with strong CYP3A4 inhibitors.
QT/QTc prolonging drugs: Reduce the dose of VANFLYTA if increases in QT interval are detected. Monitor patients more frequently with ECG if coadministration of VANFLYTA with drugs known to prolong the QT interval is required.
No clinically relevant differences in quizartinib or AC886 exposure were identified according to age, body weight, sex, race, weak or moderate CYP3A4 inhibitors, mild or moderate renal impairment, and mild or moderate hepatic impairment.
L522: Review Issue	Recommendations/Comments
Labeling	Overall, the proposed labeling recommendations are acceptable upon the Applicant's agreement to FDA's revisions.
L523: ## 6.2. Summary of Clinical Pharmacology Assessment
L524: ## 6.2.1. Pharmacology and Clinical Pharmacokinetics
L525: ## **Data:**
L526: Data are included in other sections of this document.
L527: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L528: A comprehensive clinical pharmacology program characterizing the PK of quizartinib, intrinsic and extrinsic factors on quizartinib PK, drug-drug interactions (DDIs), and exposure-response (E-R) relationships was conducted in both AML subjects and healthy subjects and provides adequate understanding of quizartinib disposition and support for the intended dosing regimen of quizartinib for the indication of treatment of adult patients with newly diagnosed *FLT3-ITD (+) AML*.
L529: The commercial tablet formulation of quizartinib provides adequate exposure levels for once daily (QD) dosing with linear PK over the intended doses. Food intake does not significantly impact the clinical PK, which is characterized by extensive metabolism and formation of the major circulating active metabolite AC886, formed via cytochrome P450 (CYP) 3A mediated oxidative metabolism. Population PK analysis showed that quizartinib PK varied during the different treatment phases in newly diagnosed AML subjects which was captured by including a phase effect on the relative bioavailability in the final model. The biological reasons for this are unknown. Quizartinib and AC886 are not inhibitors or inducers for CYP3A therefore autoinhibition or autoinduction is not anticipated.
L530: Clinically relevant E-R relationships for efficacy and safety as well as the impact of intrinsic and extrinsic factors have been characterized. They indicate a need for cardiac monitoring based on a dose-dependent increase in QT intervals, the need to avoid coadministration of moderate or strong inducers of CYP3A, and requiring a dose reduction in the case of coadministration of strong CYP3A inhibitors. Quizartinib and AC886 are not anticipated to affect the PK of other drugs through CYP enzymes. Quizartinib is a weak inhibitor of intestinal P-gp and predicted to have no to weak effect on UGT1A1 substrates. Quizartinib and AC886 have minimal potential to affect other transporters at therapeutic concentrations. Mild and moderate renal or hepatic impairment have no clinically meaningful impact on the PK of quizartinib and AC886, except that no data is available for individuals with severe renal or hepatic impairment and use in these populations is not recommended. Therapeutic individualization is not indicated based on
L531: age, sex, or race.
L532: The dosing regimen used in Study AC220-A-U302 of subjects with newly diagnosed *FLT3*-ITD (+) AML is appropriate and adequately supported by the observed improvement in OS, the observed safety profile, and the data from a comprehensive clinical pharmacology program.
L533: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L534: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position that quizartinib (free base) showed linear PK in the dose range of 26.5 mg to 79.5 mg in healthy subjects and 17.7 mg to 53 mg in patients with AML. The highest dose proposed for use in patients with AML is 53 mg and falls within the linear range.
L535: PPK analysis determined that the median quizartinib exposure (AUC_{ss}, C_{max,ss}, C_{min,ss}) was 71 to 73% higher in healthy subjects compared to subjects with AML following the same dosage.
L536: In study AC220-A-U302, the PK of quizartinib in patients with newly diagnosed AML differed across the three treatment phases (induction, consolidation, and maintenance). Quizartinib clearance was slightly lower during consolidation treatment compared to induction treatment. Following the proposed dosage of 35.4 mg once daily (QD), median quizartinib steady state exposures (AUC_{ss} and C_{max,ss}) were 40% higher during consolidation compared to induction (AUC_{ss} = 4290 versus 3086 ng\cdothr/mL; C_{max,ss} = 210 versus 151 ng/mL).
L537: Quizartinib exposure during maintenance treatment is expected to be significantly higher than exposure during induction or consolidation treatment due to two factors: 1) proposed maintenance dosage of 53 mg QD which is higher than the 35.4 mg QD dosage for induction and consolidation, and 2) significantly lower quizartinib clearance during maintenance treatment compared to induction or consolidation treatment. Following the proposed dosage in study AC220-A-U302, quizartinib median steady state exposures (AUC_{ss} and C_{max,ss}) were 266% higher during maintenance treatment compared to induction treatment (AUC_{ss} = 11286 versus 3086 ng\cdothr/mL; C_{max,ss} = 553 versus 151 ng/mL). This "phase effect" on quizartinib PK was not observed in other studies, which may be due to the design of other studies, use of quizartinib monotherapy versus combination therapy, differences in disease status over time and across studies, lower patient numbers, and limited PK samples. See Section 19.4.1.2 for additional details.
L538: See Section 6.3.1 for FDA's assessment of general pharmacology and clinical PK.
L539: See Section 6.2.2 for FDA's assessment of the proposed dosing regimen, E-R relationships of quizartinib, and therapeutic individualization.
L540: ## 6.2.2. General Dosing and Therapeutic Individualization
L541: ## 6.2.2.1. General Dosing
L542: ## **Data:**
L543: A 40-mg dose during the Induction and Consolidation Phases was selected based on the results of the Phase 1 Study 2689-CL-0005 (Altman, 2018) and Study AML18 (Burnett, 2013) in subjects with newly diagnosed AML in combination with standard chemotherapy. Both studies identified 40 mg QD for 14 days as a safe recommended dose with the potential for clinical efficacy when given in conjunction with standard chemotherapy. The 40 mg dose was further supported by data from an ex vivo plasma inhibitory activity (PIA) assay utilizing *FLT3-ITD* (+) cell lines with data from Study 2689-CL-2004 in R/R AML indicating that 30 mg is the minimum dose for complete and rapid inhibition of *FLT3* signaling in this assay. The potential impact of stromal signaling via *FLT* (the cognate *FLT3*-ligand) and resulting protection of leukemic blast cells from apoptosis (Sato, 2011; Yang, 2014) likely requires higher exposure levels, especially in the bone marrow compartment. Considering available safety data from Studies 2689-CL-0005, AML18, and AC220-007 (QuANTUM-R), the dosing regimen utilized in Study AC220-A-U302 thus represents an appropriate trade-off between safety and efficacy relevant considerations.
L544: A 2-week escalating dose schedule was implemented as a risk minimization measure to monitor dose-dependent QT prolongation and was based on QT interval corrected with Fridericia's formula (QTcF) interval data from the Phase 2 Study 2689-CL-2004. The target dose of 60 mg QD was supported by Study 2689-CL-0011 of quizartinib monotherapy as maintenance in *FLT3-ITD* (+) AML, which showed that both 40 mg QD and 60 mg QD were well tolerated, with similar incidence of Grade 3/4 adverse events (AEs) and with rapid and durable inhibition of *FLT3* at the 60 mg QD dose in the PIA.
L545: Safety and efficacy data from Study AC220-A-U302 demonstrate a significant survival benefit for the dosing regimen utilized with an adequate and manageable safety profile and a positive benefit-risk assessment when considering the overall poor outcomes in the intended patient population. A statistically significant effect of quizartinib exposure on OS was found in subjects with newly diagnosed AML, where higher exposure was associated with longer OS, supporting the overall clinical benefit of dose escalation to 60 mg. The estimated slope was relatively uncertain (relative standard error [RSE] 37%) and led to a modest E-R relationship. Quizartinib prolonged QTcF in a concentration-dependent manner. No E-R relationship was identified for the probability of the first occurrence of the investigated AEs; however, a quizartinib treatment effect was found for treatment-emergent AEs (TEAEs) leading to dose reduction/interruption, TEAEs leading to study drug discontinuation, events in the Torsade de Pointes (TdP)/QT prolongation standardized MedDRA query (SMQ), and myelosuppression.
L546: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L547: Quizartinib should be administered in combination with standard chemotherapy at a dose of 40 mg QD for 2 weeks in each cycle of induction and consolidation, starting from Day 8 of induction or Day 6 of consolidation chemotherapy. During the Continuation Phase, quizartinib should be initiated at a starting dose of 30 mg (26.5 mg free base) QD and then increased after 2 weeks of treatment to the recommended dose of 60 mg (53 mg free base) QD if corrected QTcF was \le 450 ms. Treatment with quizartinib can be continued for up to 36 cycles (28-day cycles), as long as the patient derives clinical benefit.
L548: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L549: FDA agrees with the Applicant's proposed dosing regimen of 35.4 mg (free base) once daily for the induction and the consolidation phases. Of note, the 40 mg (salt form) once daily dose described in the Applicant's text above is equal to the 35.4 mg dose (free base).
L550: FDA does not consider the Applicant's proposed dosing regimen for the maintenance phase to be sufficiently justified. See Section 6.2.2.3 for the PMR and Section 6.3.1 for FDA's assessment of the proposed dosing regimen for the continuation/maintenance phase. E-R analysis in patients who received at least one maintenance dose suggests that higher quizartinib exposure during maintenance does not result in improved survival. Given the quizartinib concentration-dependent QTcF prolongation and other safety events associated with quizartinib treatment, the proposed 53 mg once daily maintenance dose of quizartinib may expose patients to unnecessary toxicity without an improvement in survival compared to lower maintenance doses.
L551: ## 6.2.2.2. Therapeutic Individualization
L552: ## **Data:**
L553: Data are included in other sections of this document.
L554: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L555: Clinically significant DDI were seen with strong CYP3A inhibitors and moderate CYP3A inducer efavirenz. Dose reduction is required in the case of strong CYP3A inhibitors, and moderate or strong inducers of CYP3A should be avoided. Quizartinib and AC886 have minimal or no potential for clinically relevant transporter-mediated DDI and minimal potential to impact the PK of other drugs given concomitantly. Additionally, quizartinib dose should be adjusted based on QTcF with electrocardiogram (ECG) monitoring.
L556: Intrinsic factors including age, sex, and Black race, were identified in the population PK analysis as having statistically significant effects on the PK of quizartinib and AC886. However, these
L557: effects were not considered clinically meaningful. Therefore, no dose adjustment is required in any of these subpopulations. Mild or moderate renal or hepatic impairment have no clinically meaningful impact on the PK of quizartinib or AC886, and dose adjustments are not warranted. Since there is no data on subjects with severe renal or hepatic impairment, the use of quizartinib in these populations is not recommended.
L558: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L559: FDA agrees with the Applicant's positions that dose reduction is required in the presence of strong CYP3A inhibitors and that concomitant use of strong or moderate CYP3A inducers should be avoided. See Section 6.3.2.4 for specific recommendations for dosage adjustment for concomitant use of strong CYP3A inhibitors.
L560: FDA generally agrees that no dosage adjustments are necessary according to age, sex, body weight, race (in Black or African American, or, White or Asian) patients, mild or moderate hepatic impairment, mild or moderate renal impairment, or coadministration of weak or moderate CYP3A4 inhibitors.
L561: FDA does not agree with the Applicant's proposed dose modification criteria in the continuation phase. The proposed product label currently reads:
L562: (b) (4)
L563: If a QTcF &gt;500 ms is observed during induction or consolidation, the dose of quizartinib should not be escalated during the continuation phase.
L564: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment of the impact of FLT3-ITD VAF and NPM1 mutations on OS and response rate. Baseline demographics and disease features including FLT3-ITD VAF and NPM1 mutation status are described in **Table 24**, while **Figure 3** shows the impact on overall survival (OS) (Section 8.1.2). The OS hazard ratios favoring quizartinib were observed in all VAF subgroups (highest in the subgroup with &gt;50% VAF), consistent with the mechanism of action of quizartinib, and in the subgroup of patients with mutations in NPM1. This is consistent with the mechanism of action of quizartinib and with the published literature on the role of wildtype NPM1 as a negative prognostic factor. EFS and CR/CRc analyses conducted by the Applicant showed that results seen in the mutant NPM1 and wildtype NPM1 subgroups are consistent with the overall study population (**Table 48** and **Table 49**). The Applicant's exploratory subgroup analyses suggest a benefit on EFS and no suggestion of harm for other endpoints which, taken together with the high unmet medical need in NPM1 wildtype patients, supports approval in both subgroups. EFS hazard ratios of 0.760 in the wildtype NPM1
L565: subgroup and 0.696 in the mutant NPM1 subgroup, suggest that there may be benefit of quizartinib treatment regardless of NPM1 status (Section 8.1.2).
L566: ## 6.2.2.3. Outstanding Issues
L567: ## The Applicant's Position:
L568: None.
L569: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L570: There is one outstanding question regarding whether the proposed dosing regimen for the maintenance phase is appropriate:
L571: The randomized, controlled trial, QuANTUM-First, used planned maintenance dosage of 26.5 mg quizartinib once daily (QD) for two weeks followed by dose escalation to 53 mg QD if QTcF remained less than or equal to 450 ms. Maintenance treatment was to continue for up to 36 months. Subjects were randomized to quizartinib or placebo treatment before starting induction treatment but were not re-randomized prior to starting maintenance treatment. As a result, it is uncertain whether safety and efficacy outcomes, including overall survival, were impacted by maintenance quizartinib dose because the effect(s) of quizartinib dose and exposure during maintenance cannot be evaluated independently of the effects of quizartinib dose and exposure during induction and consolidation.
L572: Additionally, the quizartinib dose and exposure during maintenance were directly impacted by QTcF interval due to the QTcF-dependent dose escalation. Factors associated with QTcF, including age and other patient and disease characteristics, may also confound the analysis of maintenance efficacy and safety according to quizartinib maintenance dose and exposure.
L573: The E-R analyses did not identify any clear associations between OS or EFS and higher average quizartinib exposure from first maintenance dose up to time of event/censoring (see detailed analysis in Section 19.4.3.2). However, higher quizartinib exposure is associated with greater QTcF prolongation and may put patients at risk of a worse safety profile. The recommended 53 mg QD quizartinib maintenance dosage is expected to result in over 2-fold higher quizartinib exposure (AUC, Cmax, Ctrough) compared to the recommended 35.4 mg QD induction and consolidation dosage (see detailed analysis in Section 19.4.1.2). Therefore, the recommended quizartinib maintenance dosage may result in a worse safety profile without improvements to overall survival compared to lower maintenance dosages.
L574: Based on the available information, the risk/benefit profile of the inclusion of quizartinib maintenance therapy, and the appropriate dose for maintenance therapy, are unclear. Additional information is necessary to determine a clinically beneficial maintenance dosage that
L575: does not expose subjects to unnecessary toxicity or safety events and is tolerable. The additional data would address the known serious risks of QTcF interval prolongation, neutropenia, and other signals of serious risk related to the use of quizartinib in the maintenance setting.
L576: FDA will issue the following PMR:
L577: Conduct a randomized trial of quizartinib maintenance therapy that compares the recommended dosage of 53 mg daily to a lower dosage (e.g., 26.5 mg daily) to further characterize serious adverse reactions including but not limited to the rates of Grade \ge 3 adverse reactions, Grade \ge 3 neutropenia, QTcF interval prolongation, and dose reductions, interruptions, and discontinuations due to adverse reactions and provide an analysis of dose- and exposure-response relationships for safety. Incorporate systematically assessed patient-reported outcome assessments to further characterize safety and tolerability, including patient-reported symptoms, function, and overall side effect impact. Eligible patients will include newly-diagnosed, FLT3-ITD positive AML patients in complete remission following consolidation chemotherapy. The study should also provide an analysis of dose- and exposure-response relationships for efficacy, including overall survival and relapse-free survival.

L596: ## The Applicant's Position:
L597: The pharmacology and PK of quizartinib and its active metabolite AC886 are well characterized.
L598: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L599: FDA generally agrees with the pharmacology and PK characteristics presented in **Table 11** with the exception of the dose proportionality range reported for patients with AML. Quizartinib exposure (AUC and C_{max}) increased proportionally in the dose range of 17.7 mg to 53 mg (free base) in patients with AML, and not 17.1 to 53 mg which is mistakenly reported in **Table 11**.
L600: E-R analysis of QTcF prolongation indicated that higher quizartinib concentrations are associated with greater increases in QTcF interval. Clearance is expected to be lower during maintenance treatment compared to induction or consolidation, and maintenance quizartinib exposure is predicted to be higher than induction or consolidation quizartinib exposure following the proposed dosage. In a typical subject in Study AC220-A-U302, the median QTcF interval prolongation is expected to be +18.4 msec (90% CI 16.3 to 20.5 msec) at the maximum concentration at steady state (C_{max,ss}) following 26.5 mg QD during maintenance treatment and +24.1 msec (90% CI 21.4 to 26.6 msec) at C_{max,ss} following 53 mg QD during maintenance treatment. See Section 19.4.3.4 for the detailed analysis of quizartinib concentration impact on QTcF prolongation.
L601: ## 6.3.2. **Clinical Pharmacology Questions**
L602: ## 6.3.2.1. **Does the clinical pharmacology program provide supportive evidence of effectiveness?**
L603: ## The Applicant's Position:
L604: Yes. Ex vivo pharmacodynamic (PD) data (PIA) from Study 2689-CL-2004 show rapid and near complete inhibition at 30 mg QD and 60 mg QD, supporting that adequate target coverage can be expected under the proposed dosing regimen (Nepomuceno, 2014). Additionally, results from the E-R analysis for efficacy provide additional supporting information (see Sections 6.2.2.1 and 19.4.3.1).
L605: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L606: The primary evidence of effectiveness is the analysis of OS in Study AC220-A-U302 which is described in Section 8.1. Supportive evidence of effectiveness from the clinical pharmacology program is summarized below.
L607: Using blood samples collected under protocol 2689-2004, an ex-vivo pharmacodynamic assay was conducted evaluating the inhibition of phosphorylation of FLT3 using plasma inhibitory assay (PIA) and STAT5. The assay was conducted on 47 samples collected day 8 and 15 samples collected day 15 of quizartinib treatment. Administration of either 26.5 or 53 mg/day quizartinib resulted in plasma levels that resulted in near complete FLT3-ITD inhibitory activity as measured by the PIA assay. Expansion of the assay to evaluate inhibition of FLT3-WT and KIT phosphorylation activity demonstrated reduced effects of quizartinib at both doses on these kinases compared to FLT3-ITD, with notably large reductions in KIT inhibitory activity.
L608: The E-R analysis of efficacy supports the conclusion that quizartinib induction and consolidation is effective. After accounting for the impact of age on OS, the E-R analysis found that improved OS may be associated with either higher quizartinib exposure during induction treatment or treatment with quizartinib (versus placebo) (refer to Section 19.4.3.2 for detailed analysis).
L609: The E-R analysis does not provide evidence of the effectiveness of quizartinib maintenance treatment. The maintenance E-R analysis identified that improved OS was associated with lower age and HSCT in subjects who received at least one dose of maintenance treatment. However, no significant associations were identified between OS and induction exposure, maintenance exposure, and/or treatment arm (i.e. quizartinib versus placebo). The E-R analyses are described in detail in Section 19.4.3.
L610: ## **6.3.2.2. Is the proposed dosing regimen appropriate for the general patient population for which the indication is being sought?**
L611: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L612: Yes. Study AC220-A-U302 enrolled adult subjects from 18-75 years of age with FLT3-ITD (+) newly diagnosed AML and demonstrated a clinically meaningful improvement in survival in the overall population with a favorable benefit-risk assessment. Population PK analyses did not indicate body weight, age, sex, race, or other factors to result in clinically meaningful changes in exposure that would warrant dose adjustments. See also Section 6.3.2.3.
L613: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L614: The E-R analysis of efficacy supports the conclusion that the proposed 35.4 mg once daily dose
L615: of quizartinib during induction and consolidation is acceptable from a clinical pharmacology perspective.
L616: For maintenance treatment, the E-R analysis does not provide evidence that the proposed quizartinib maintenance dosage of 53 mg once daily is appropriate. The maintenance E-R analysis indicates that improved OS was associated with lower age and HSCT in subjects who received at least one dose of maintenance treatment. However, improved OS was not associated with induction exposure, maintenance exposure, or quizartinib treatment (versus placebo) in subjects who received at least one dose of maintenance treatment.
L617: Safety analyses indicate that quizartinib is associated with safety events and that higher quizartinib concentrations are associated with greater QTcF prolongation. Therefore, E-R analyses suggests that the proposed maintenance dose of 53 mg once daily may expose patients to unnecessary toxicity without improving survival compared to lower maintenance doses. A PMR will address the need for additional data to determine an acceptable quizartinib maintenance dosage (refer to Section 6.2.2.3). The maintenance E-R analysis is described in detail in Section 19.4.3.2.
L618: ## 6.3.2.3. Is an alternative dosing regimen or management strategy required for subpopulations based on intrinsic patient factors?
L619: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L620: No. Intrinsic factors, including age, sex, and race, may have an impact on quizartinib PK, but the changes seen in the population PK analysis are not clinically meaningful and do not warrant any dose adjustments. Mild or moderate renal or hepatic impairment have limited or no potential to impact the elimination of quizartinib or AC886, and dose adjustments are not warranted. Since there is no data on patients with severe renal or hepatic impairment, the use of quizartinib in these populations is not recommended.
L621: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L622: FDA agrees that no clinically meaningful differences in exposure are expected according to body weight, age, race (Black or African American, White, or Asian), mild or moderate hepatic impairment, and mild or moderate renal impairment. Of note, there was limited representation of racial subgroups other than White (65%), Asian (18%), or Black or African American (9%), and it is unknown if the PK of quizartinib or AC886 differs in other racial subgroups.
L623: Body weight and race were associated with statistically significant impacts on quizartinib clearance. The median quizartinib AUCss, Cmax,ss, and Cmin,ss are 22 to 23% higher in a typical
L624: patient weighing 65 kg compared to a typical patient weighing 85 kg, but this exposure difference is not expected to have a significant impact on clinical outcomes. The PPK model also predicted that Black and African American subjects would have 30 to 35% higher median AUC_{ss} and C_{max,ss} compared to subjects of other races, but E-R analysis did not find any E-R safety associations where 30 to 35% higher exposure would result in higher rates of TEAEs. Therefore, Black and African American patients with previously untreated AML are not expected to have clinically significant differences in quizartinib exposure compared to patients of other races. See Section 19.4.1.2 for detailed analysis of covariate effects on exposure.
L625: **Renal Impairment:** The results of the ADME study (AC220-006) showed that &lt;2% of the total dose is excreted renally. Accordingly, renal impairment is expected to have a negligible impact on quizartinib elimination. This relationship between creatinine clearance and circulating drug concentrations was evaluated using population PK methods. Data from 932 subjects (273 healthy subjects and 659 acute myeloid leukemia (AML) patients) were included in the PPK model. Creatinine clearance (estimated by the Cockcroft-Gault equation) was assessed as a covariate in the PPK analysis on quizartinib and AC886 clearance. Creatinine clearance in subjects with mild and moderate renal impairment was not found to be a statistically significant covariate on quizartinib and AC886 clearance (PPK Report AC220-PMx009). There were insufficient data from subjects with severe renal impairment to draw meaningful conclusions regarding the impact of severe renal impairment on quizartinib clearance (n = 4 [0.4%]). Based on the PPK analyses, quizartinib dose adjustment is not required in patients with mild or moderate renal impairment. Data were insufficient to draw conclusions regarding the impact of severe renal impairment. Consequently, administration of quizartinib in subjects with severe renal impairment is not recommended.
L626: **Hepatic Impairment:** There have been two studies evaluating the impact of hepatic impairment on quizartinib exposure.
L627: Study AC220-016 was conducted in subjects with mild (n=8), moderate (n=8) hepatic impairment based on Child-Pugh classification, and healthy subjects (n=14) who received a single oral dose of 26.5 mg quizartinib. Noncompartmental analysis (NCA) of the PK data showed that mild hepatic impairment has no significant effect on quizartinib and AC886 exposures (**Table 12**). The effect of moderate hepatic impairment had no significant effect on quizartinib exposure, but resulted in a 35% decrease in AC886 exposure (**Table 12**).

L636: Source: AC220-019 Final Study Report; Table 9.1, 9.2, 9.3, 9.4; Pages 55, 56, 58, 59
L637: In cancer patients, hepatic function is routinely classified using the NCI-ODWG criteria. Evaluation of the liver scores in individual subjects showed that the ascites score was the main contributor to the classification of subjects into either the mild or moderate liver impairment categories for Child-Pugh. Based on the large difference in the number of subjects considered healthy subjects or impaired based on the liver classification system, and the high risk of QTcF prolongation, small changes in exposure may lead to potential risk of serious adverse events. As a result, data using the NCI-ODWG classification criteria were considered as they are more conservative.
L638: The hepatic impairment subject population was re-evaluated using the NCI-ODWG criteria. This resulted in 7 additional subjects being considered to have normal liver function, 8 subjects meeting the criteria for mild hepatic impairment, and only a single subject meeting the criteria for moderate hepatic impairment. Repeating the analysis using the NCI-ODWG classification system showed that the effect of mild hepatic impairment has no clinically meaningful effect on quizartinib and AC886 exposure. In addition, a second study was conducted, enrolling subjects that met the NCI-ODWG criteria for moderate hepatic impairment.
L639: Study AC220-A-U105 was conducted in subjects with moderate (n=6) and healthy subjects (n=6) based on NCI-ODWG criteria. The NCA showed that total quizartinib Cmax, AUClast, and AUCinf were comparable for subjects with moderate hepatic impairment and control healthy subjects; geometric mean ratios were 95.1%, 109%, and 109%, respectively. The unbound fraction (fu) of quizartinib at 4 hours post dose was lower in subjects with moderate hepatic impairment (mean of 0.00452, ranging from 0.00112 to 0.0108) compared with control subjects (mean of 0.00900, ranging from 0.00318 to 0.0180). The mean unbound Cmax and AUCinf of quizartinib were 55% and 49% lower, respectively, in subjects with
L640: moderate hepatic impairment compared to control subjects.
L641: These results should be interpreted cautiously as the mechanism for the decrease in free (unbound) drug in patients with moderate hepatic impairment is not well explained at this time. In AC220-A-U105, there were 17 PK samples per patient (~204 PK samples) available for use in the total drug AUC calculation. C_{max} and AUC for unbound drug were extrapolated from a single free drug determination per patient (~12 samples) using the 4 hour post dose PK sample. Quizartinib is over 99% protein bound, so the unbound fraction is quite small. Assay variability and the small sample size may have influenced this finding. **Table 13** summarizes quizartinib PK in AC220-A-U105.
L642: Active metabolite (AC886) C_{max} and AUC_{inf} were 25% and 30% lower, respectively, in subjects with moderate hepatic impairment compared to control subjects. Mean t_{1/2} was longer for subjects with moderate hepatic impairment (111.1 \pm 48.7 hours) compared to control subjects (81.4 \pm 20.7 hours).
L643: Moderate hepatic impairment does not significantly impact quizartinib exposure. Dose adjustment for mild or moderate hepatic impairment is not required.

L649: ## 6.3.2.4. Are there clinically relevant food-drug or drug-drug interactions, and what is the appropriate management strategy?
L650: ## The Applicant's Position:
L651: Yes. Strong CYP3A inhibitors result in a clinically significant increase in exposure and warrant a
L652: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 216993}
## {VANFLYTA; quizartinib}
L653: dose reduction to 50% of the regular dose when quizartinib is given concomitantly with strong CYP3A inhibitors. Moderate or strong inducers of CYP3A have the potential to impact the exposure to quizartinib and AC886 and should be avoided. There were no clinically meaningful changes to quizartinib or AC886 plasma exposures when coadministered with food or gastric acid-reducing agents (ARAs). Quizartinib can be coadministered with gastric ARAs and without regard to food.
L654: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L655: ## Food Effect:
L656: The impact of a high-fat (50%), high calorie (approximately 150, 250, and 500 to 600 calories from protein, carbohydrate, and fat, respectively, for a total of 1000 calories meal) on quizartinib and AC886 PK was evaluated in 63 healthy subjects administered a single oral 26.5 mg dose of quizartinib in Study AC220-019. No significant change in AUC or C_{max} was observed in the fed compared to the fasted subjects. Median T_{max} was 2 hours longer for quizartinib and 31 hours longer for AC886 when quizartinib was given with a high-fat meal compared to the fasted state (**Table 14**). These results support the labeling recommendation of administering quizartinib with or without food.

L662: ## Effect of Strong and Moderate CYP3A4 inhibitors:
L663: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 216993}
## {VANFLYTA; quizartinib}
L664: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's position that dose reductions are warranted when quizartinib is given concomitantly with a strong CYP3A inhibitor. It is noted that during the first two weeks of maintenance a 33% (instead of 50%) dose reduction from 26.5 to 17.7 mg is recommended. The following dosage modifications are recommended:
L665: - If the dose being administered is 35.4 mg, then the dose is reduced to 17.7 mg in the presence of a strong CYP3A inhibitor.
- If the dose being administered is 26.5 mg, then the dose is reduced to 17.7 mg in the presence of a strong CYP3A inhibitor.
- If the dose being administered is 53 mg, then the dose is reduced to 26.5 mg in the presence of a strong CYP3A inhibitor.
L666: Based on results from Study AC220-015, no clinically significant differences in quizartinib exposure are expected with concomitant use of moderate CYP3A inhibitors.
L667: In Study AC220-015, the effect of fluconazole, a moderate CYP3A inhibitor, or ketoconazole, a strong CYP3A inhibitor, on quizartinib and AC886 PK was evaluated using an open-label, randomized, placebo-controlled, parallel design study. Three groups of 31 healthy subjects each were evaluated for PK interactions:
L668: Group 1	Ketoconazole 200 mg twice daily on Days 1 through 28	Quizartinib 26.5 mg single dose on Day 8
Group 2	Fluconazole 200 mg twice daily on Days 1 through 28	Quizartinib 26.5 mg single dose on Day 8
Group 3	Placebo twice daily on Days 1 through 28	Quizartinib 26.5 mg single dose on Day 8
L669: Repeated coadministration of ketoconazole resulted in a 94% increase in quizartinib geometric mean AUC_{0-inf} and a 17% increase in quizartinib geometric mean C_{max}. Active metabolite AC886 geometric mean AUC_{0-inf} decreased by 15%, and its geometric mean C_{max} decreased by 60%. The time to reach maximal AC886 concentration was prolonged indicating that CYP3A4-mediated conversion of quizartinib to AC886 was inhibited. These changes are considered clinically relevant drug-drug interaction at steady-state for both exposure parameters.
L670: Fluconazole coadministration had a lesser impact on quizartinib exposure. There was an 18% increase in quizartinib AUC_{0-504h} and an 11% increase in quizartinib C_{max}. The AC886 AUC_{0-504h} increased by 12% and AC886 C_{max} increased by 2%. The changes in exposure following fluconazole administration were not considered to be clinically significant.
L671: **Table 15** and **Table 16** summarize PK in Study AC220-015.

L680: Effect of Strong and Moderate CYP3A4 Inducers:
L681: Study AC220-A-U106 determined that quizartinib AUC up to infinity (AUC_{0-inf}) decreased by 90% and C_{max} decreased by 45% following a single 53 mg dose of quizartinib with concomitant use of efavirenz (a moderate CYP3A inducer). The AUC_{0-inf} of AC886 decreased by 96% and the AC886 C_{max} decreased by 68%. The PPK data is inadequate to assess the impact of weak, moderate, or strong CYP3A inducer coadministration on quizartinib or AC886 PK. Due to the potential for decreased effectiveness with lower quizartinib exposure, FDA agrees with the Applicant's position to avoid concomitant use of strong or moderate CYP3A inducers.
L682: ## Effect of Gastric Acid Reducing Agents:
L683: Study AC220-018 evaluated the effect of a proton pump inhibitor (PPI), lansoprazole, 60 mg on days 1-5 on quizartinib and AC886 PK following a single oral dose of 26.5 mg quizartinib in 62 healthy subjects. Concomitant administration of lansoprazole had no meaningful effect on quizartinib AUC, C_{max} and T_{max} compared to quizartinib alone (Table 17). These results suggest that quizartinib can be taken concomitantly with PPIs without adjusting the quizartinib dose.

L730: NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 216993}
{VANFLYTA; quizartinib}
L731: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L732: As of 06 May 2022, the quizartinib clinical program consisted of 26 clinical studies conducted by the Applicant, including 13 studies in either newly diagnosed or R/R AML, 1 study in solid tumors, and 12 single-dose studies in healthy subjects and subjects with hepatic impairment. Of the 13 studies in subjects with AML, 10 completed studies are included in the evaluation of the safety and efficacy of quizartinib (3 in newly diagnosed AML, 1 in maintenance monotherapy after HSCT, and 6 in R/R AML). A pediatric study, AC220-A-U202, in R/R *FLT3*-ITD (+) AML (see Section 8.2.9) and a foreign study, AC220-A-A103, in Chinese subjects with newly diagnosed AML are ongoing studies and were excluded from the NDA. Additionally, Study DS3032-A-U105 was excluded as it was a combination study with milademetan and thus, was not using the target quizartinib treatment regimen.
L733: The studies summarized in Table 18 provide the basis for the evaluation of clinical pharmacology, efficacy and safety of quizartinib in the indicated population with Study AC220-A-U302 providing the pivotal data supporting safety and efficacy of quizartinib in the proposed indication. Nine additional studies support the safety of quizartinib and are included in the All AML Pool and an additional 14 studies provide additional information pertinent to this application. The doses of quizartinib administered in these studies ranged from 12 mg to 450 mg QD, with treatment duration of up to 241 weeks.
L734: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L735: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of their Clinical Studies Relevant to this Application.
L736: The FDA review was based on data from NDA 216993, relevant published literature, and relevant information in the public domain.
L737: Of note, Study AC220-A-U302 is the pivotal study that was the basis for establishing the safety and efficacy of quizartinib in newly diagnosed *FLT3*-ITD mutated patients. The Applicant submitted a complete dataset for this study, using a data cut date of August 13, 2021, at the time of the initial NDA submission (August 24, 2022). This data set will form the basis for this clinical review.
L738: At the time of the NDA submission, the Applicant submitted complete datasets from 9 additional studies in patients with AML, 1 study in patients with solid tumors, and 12 completed studies in healthy volunteers (Table 18). The additional studies in patients with AML provide supporting evidence of efficacy, particularly Study AC220-007. Data from all of the additional studies were used to supplement the analysis of safety.
L739: The Applicant submitted an updated data set for Study AC220-A-U302, using a data cut date of
L740: 04/12/2022, at the time of the 90-day safety update (SN0011; submitted to eCTD on 11/18/2022).
L741: Summaries of data and statistical analyses by the reviewer were performed using JMP 16.0, R Statistical Software (version 3.6.3; R Core Team 2020), and SAS 9.4 (SAS Institute, Inc. Cary, NC). MedDRA Adverse Events Diagnostic Version 3.5 (MAED) (FDA, Silver Spring, MD) was used to look for safety signals.
L742: ## 8 Statistical and Clinical Evaluation
L743: ## 8.1. Review of Relevant Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy
L744: The clinical development program of quizartinib in AML subjects consists of 13 clinical studies, 10 of which supported efficacy. Five Phase 1 studies and three Phase 2 studies examining the efficacy of quizartinib at different dose levels in subjects with *FLT3*-ITD (+) have been completed. Two large pivotal Phase 3 studies were conducted: Study AC220-007 in patients with R/R *FLT3*-ITD (+) AML and Study AC220-A-U302 in patients with newly diagnosed *FLT3*-ITD (+) AML.
L745: The efficacy of quizartinib in the treatment of patients with newly diagnosed *FLT3*-ITD (+) AML is based on the data from a single pivotal Phase 3 study AC220-A-U302 (N = 539) in newly diagnosed AML subjects, with supplemental data from the three Phase 1 studies, 2689-CL-0005 (N = 18; newly diagnosed AML), AC220-A-J102 (N = 7; newly diagnosed AML), and 2689-CL-0011 (N = 13; post-HSCT AML), for the safety and preliminary efficacy of quizartinib in this indication.
L746: In addition to the studies mentioned above, the results from Studies AC220-007, AC220-002, 2689-CL-2004, CP0001, AC220-A-J101, and AC220-A-J201 provide additional supporting data pertinent to the use of quizartinib in patients with AML and are described in the sections below.
L747: ## 8.1.1. Pivotal Study in Newly Diagnosed *FLT3*-ITD (+) AML - Study AC220-A-U302 (QuANTUM-First)
L748: The primary evaluation of efficacy of quizartinib in the treatment of patients with newly diagnosed *FLT3*-ITD (+) AML is based on the data from the single pivotal Phase 3 study, AC220-A-U302 (N=539).
L749: ## Trial Design
L750: ## The Applicant's Description:
L751: Study AC220-A-U302 was a Phase 3, randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, global study to compare the effect of quizartinib versus placebo (administered with standard induction and consolidation chemotherapy, then administered as continuation therapy for up to 36 cycles) on the primary endpoint of OS in subjects 18 to 75 years of age with newly diagnosed *FLT3*-ITD (+) AML. The study design from the Screening Visit through the Treatment Period is shown in Figure 1. The Long-term Follow-up Phase was to begin upon completion of 36 cycles of study drug (quizartinib/placebo) in the Continuation Phase or permanent discontinuation of study drug in any phase.
L752: **Figure 1 – Study Design Schematic (Screening to Treatment Period) for Study AC220-A-U302**
L753: a During Induction Cycle 2 investigators may choose to administer the “7+3” chemotherapy regimen, or the “5+2” chemotherapy regimen, and quizartinib/placebo will therefore start on Day 8 or Day 6, respectively.
L754: Following the disclosure within the RATIFY study of most relapses occurring within 24 months, the duration of the Continuation Phase was increased to 36 cycles. Patients were appropriately informed of available treatment options.
L755: **Trial Location:** This study was conducted at 193 study sites across 26 countries in North America, Europe, and Asia/Other Regions.
L756: **Treatment assignment:** 539 subjects were enrolled and randomized to one of the two treatment arms (quizartinib arm or placebo arm) in a 1:1 ratio through a stratified permuted block randomization procedure by an Interactive Voice/Web Response System (IXRS). Randomization was stratified by region (North America, Europe, Asia/Other Regions), age (&lt;60 years old, \ge 60 years old), and WBC count at the time of diagnosis of AML (&lt;40 \times 10^9/L, \ge 40 \times 10^9/L).
L757: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 216993}
## {VANFLYTA; quizartinib}
L758: **Blinding:** This is a double-blind placebo-controlled study. Neither the subjects nor any of the investigators, Applicant, or contract research organizations (CROs) were aware of the quizartinib/placebo treatments received. During the course of the prior NDA review, 5 patients were unblinded at the request of the FDA, all of whom received quizartinib.
L759: **Study treatment:** During the first induction cycle, patients received a standard 7+3 induction regimen with cytarabine 100 mg/m2/day (200 mg/m2/day allowed if institutional or local standard) by continuous intravenous (IV) infusion from Days 1 to 8 and anthracycline (daunorubicin 60 mg/m2/day or idarubicin 12 mg/m2/day) on Days 1, 2, and 3. Study drug (quizartinib 40 mg or placebo) was to be administered QD starting on Day 8 (at the end of the cytarabine infusion) for 14 consecutive days. Subjects could receive either a second cycle of identical induction chemotherapy or a five-day infusion of cytarabine and 2 daily doses of daunorubicin or idarubicin (5 + 2) according to institutional routine practice followed by 14 days of quizartinib 40 mg (or placebo) QD.
L760: Patients achieving CR or CRi at the end of induction could proceed to consolidation, consisting of (1) treatment with HDAC (3.0 g/m2 IV infusion every 12 hours on Days 1, 3, and 5, reduced to 1.5 g/m2 for patients aged ≥60 years) followed by quizartinib (40 mg/day) or placebo for 14 days starting on Day 6 of each cycle (up to 4 cycles), or (2) allo-HSCT only (between the end of induction to within 3 months of starting continuation), or (3) treatment with HDAC followed by quizartinib (40 mg/day) or placebo followed by allo-HSCT.
L761: Continuation monotherapy with quizartinib/placebo was to begin after induction and consolidation therapy upon blood count recovery. In the Continuation Phase, the dose of quizartinib/placebo started at 30 mg orally QD and increased to 60 mg/day on Cycle 1 Day 16 if the average QTcF of the triplicate ECG was ≤450 ms. Study drug continued for up to 36 cycles or until relapse, start of non-protocol-specified AML treatment, death, unacceptable toxicity, or study closure, whichever occurred first.
L762: For subjects concomitantly receiving a strong CYP3A4 inhibitor, the dose of quizartinib was to be reduced from 40 to 20 mg QD during the Induction and Consolidation Phases and from 30 to 20 mg QD or from 60 to 30 mg QD during the Continuation Phase.
L763: **Duration of treatment:** Total duration of treatment with study drug was up to 42 cycles (inclusive of the Induction, Consolidation, and Continuation Phases). All subjects who completed 36 cycles of quizartinib/placebo in the Continuation Phase or permanently discontinued study drug in any phase entered the Long-term Follow-up Phase.
L764: **Choice of the control arm:** At the time of initiation of the global, multicenter study AC220-A-U302 (Sep 2016), chemotherapy with or without allo-HSCT was the standard for the treatment of AML. Hence, placebo along with standard combination chemotherapy was used as the comparator arm in the study.
L765: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 216993}
## {VANFLYTA; quizartinib}
L766: In 2017, the multikinase Type I FLT3 inhibitor midostaurin was approved in the US for the treatment of newly diagnosed *FLT3*-TKD (+) or *FLT3*-ITD (+) AML based on the results of the RATIFY study (Stone, 2017). Approval status and accessibility varied globally following the US approval with limited availability of midostaurin in the European Union and Asia/Pacific regions, from which the majority of subjects were enrolled. At the time of this approval, screening/enrollment of Study AC220-A-U302 was well underway. As such, modifying the comparator from placebo to midostaurin in this global study would have significantly delayed critical results for an urgently needed treatment option for patients with newly diagnosed *FLT3*-ITD (+) AML. In this context, it is important to note that nonclinical and prior clinical data suggested that the second-generation, highly selective, Type II *FLT3* inhibitor quizartinib may result in improved clinical activity for patients with newly diagnosed *FLT3*-ITD (+) AML (Cortes, 2019; Aikawa, 2020) and Study AC220-A-U302 was conducted to clinically test this hypothesis in patients up to 75 years of age.
L767: The Applicant consulted the scientific steering committee, medical experts in AML, and ethicists, who concluded that equipoise was maintained and the study should continue as a placebo-controlled study. The recommendation was based on the assessment that newly diagnosed *FLT3*-ITD (+) AML remains a poor prognostic and aggressive disease with a high unmet medical need, and that advancing a next-generation *FLT3* inhibitor would provide patients the opportunity to gain access to additional treatment options. Given the poor prognosis of patients with *FLT3*-ITD (+) AML, the high risk of relapse in this population, and the limited global availability of midostaurin in key regions, no changes in the control arm of the Study AC220-A-U302 were implemented.
L768: *Dose modification/dose discontinuation:* Guidelines for dose delay or dose modification and treatment discontinuation in response to specific AEs were specified in the study protocol. Study drug dose reduction or dose interruption were to be implemented for QTcF prolongation, other nonhematologic toxicities, myelosuppression, and concomitant administration of a strong CYP3A4 inhibitor. The minimum dose of study drug was not to be lower than 30 mg/day in any phase of the study in general and not lower than 20 mg/day for subjects receiving strong CYP3A4 inhibitors. If study drug was interrupted, missed doses were not to be made up.
L769: *Concomitant medications:* Subjects were not allowed to receive concomitant chemotherapy, immunotherapy, radiotherapy, transplant, or any ancillary therapy for AML that was not specified in the protocol or that was considered to be investigational (ie, used for nonapproved indication[s] and in the context of a research investigation) while on study drug.
L770: Medications associated with QT/QTc prolongation were prohibited. Exceptions were made for antibiotics, antivirals, and antifungals that were used as standard of care for the prevention or treatment of infections or if the investigator believed that beginning therapy with a potentially QTc-prolonging medication (such as antiemetic) was vital to an individual subject's care while on study. In that case, additional ECG monitoring was to be performed at the commencement
L771: of such a medication or from when there was an increase in its dosage. Other drugs prohibited were strong or moderate CYP3A4 inducers.
L772: Strong CYP3A4 inhibitors were to be avoided, if possible, but if necessary for subject care, strong CYP3A4 inhibitors were allowed with a corresponding dose reduction of study drug. There were no restrictions for moderate or weak CYP3A4 inhibitors.
L773: **Diagnostic Criteria:** Subjects were enrolled based on *FLT3*-ITD (+) status as determined centrally using the [REDACTED] (b) (4) *FLT3*-ITD clinical trial assay (CTA) for the determination of *FLT3*-ITD mutation status from bone marrow and/or blood samples. This CTA is a molecular assay that employs polymerase chain reaction (PCR) and capillary electrophoresis technology. The Applicant also collaborated with Invivoscribe Inc., to develop a PCR and capillary electrophoresis-based molecular assay for detecting *FLT3*-ITD mutations as an in vitro diagnostic assay for a companion diagnostic.
L774: **Inclusion/Exclusion criteria:** The study enrolled subjects with newly diagnosed *FLT3*-ITD (+) AML.
L775: **Key inclusion criteria:**
L776: - Newly diagnosed, morphologically documented primary AML or AML secondary to myelodysplastic syndrome or a myeloproliferative neoplasm, based on the World Health Organization (WHO) 2008 classification (at screening)
- ≥18 years or the minimum legal adult age (whichever was greater) and ≤75 years (at screening) and receiving standard induction chemotherapy regimen as per protocol
- Presence of *FLT3*-ITD activating mutation in bone marrow (≥3% *FLT3*-ITD/total *FLT3*)
- Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group (ECOG) PS 0 to 2 (at the time the subject signed his/her first informed consent form)
- Adequate renal function (creatinine clearance &gt;50 mL/min), adequate hepatic function (total serum bilirubin ≤1.5 × upper limit of normal [ULN]; serum alkaline phosphatase [ALP], aspartate aminotransferase [AST] and alanine aminotransferase [ALT] ≤2.5 × ULN), and serum electrolytes within the institution's normal limits at the time of randomization (Day 7 of induction chemotherapy)
L777: **Key exclusion criteria:**
L778: - Diagnosis of acute promyelocytic leukemia (APL), French-American-British (FAB) classification M3 or WHO classification of APL with translocation, t(15;17)(q22;q12), or *BCR-ABL*-positive leukemia (ie, chronic myelogenous leukemia in blast crisis)
- Diagnosis of AML secondary to prior chemotherapy or radiotherapy for other neoplasms
- History of known central nervous system (CNS) leukemia, including cerebrospinal fluid positive for AML blasts; lumbar puncture was recommended for subjects with symptoms of CNS leukemia to rule out extramedullary CNS involvement
- QTcF &gt;450 ms or diagnosis of or suspicion of long QT syndrome (LQTS; including family history of LQTS); or uncontrolled or significant cardiovascular disease (eg, history of
L779: congestive heart failure [CHF], uncontrolled hypertension, and history of clinically relevant ventricular arrhythmia)
L780: *Study assessments:* During the Induction Phase, a bone marrow aspirate specimen and blood samples for hematology (complete blood count [CBC]) were collected on Day 21 (or up to Day 28) of Cycle 1 and Cycle 2. The investigator assessed the subject's response based on the local laboratory results using the modified International Working Group response criteria for AML (Cheson, 2003).
L781: Starting on Day 21, blood samples for hematology (CBC) were also collected at least weekly to assess count recovery. If an accurate assessment of response could not be made based on the Day 21 bone marrow aspirate, then the bone marrow aspirate was to be repeated upon count recovery or Day 56 (\pm 3 days), whichever occurred first.
L782: CR was defined as an absolute neutrophil count (ANC) of \ge 1000 cells/mm^3, a platelet count of \ge 100,000 cells/mm^3, and bone marrow with &lt;5\% blasts. CRI was defined as meeting all the criteria for CR with incomplete hematological recovery with residual neutropenia (ANC &lt;1000 cells/mm^3) and/or platelet count &lt;100,000 cells/mm^3. There should be no blasts with Auer rods or the presence of extramedullary disease (eg, CNS or soft tissue involvement).
L783: During the Continuation Phase, a bone marrow aspirate specimen for relapse assessment and blood samples for hematology (CBC) were collected, and physical examination (including an evaluation for signs and symptoms of relapse) was performed at the visits specified in the study protocol. If relapse was suspected based on abnormal peripheral smears or development of cytopenias at any time during the Continuation Phase, a bone marrow aspirate specimen was to be collected for analysis of response/remission.
L784: During the study treatment, subjects were monitored regularly in all phases for toxicities, including triplicate ECG for central reading, laboratory tests, electrolytes, and hematology monitoring. Dose modifications were required if the subject experienced QTcF prolongation based on study-supplied ECG machine read, other nonhematologic toxicities, or myelosuppression or had received concomitant strong CYP3A4 inhibitors during treatment.
L785: The Long-term Follow-up Phase began upon completion of 36 cycles of study drug in the Continuation Phase or upon permanent discontinuation of study drug in any phase. During this phase, subjects were regularly assessed for relapse and survival status.
L786: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L787: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of the trial design of the phase 3 Study AC220-A-U302. However, we note that there are typos in the study schema and text above describing the duration of continuous cytarabine infusion. The duration of continuous cytarabine was 7 days not 8 as noted in the text and in the study diagram above. Treatment with quizartinib or placebo would begin on day 8 after the completion of the cytarabine infusion.
L788: FDA notes that the Applicant's definition of CR response is different from that specified in FDA's Guidance for Industry *Acute Myeloid Leukemia: Developing Drugs and Biological Products for Treatment*, which specifies ANC &gt; 1000 cells/mm3 and platelets &gt; 100,000 cells/mm3 (as opposed to \ge the respective cutoffs).
L789: ## Study Endpoints
L790: ## The Applicant's Description:
L791: The primary endpoint for Study AC220-A-U302 was OS defined as the time from randomization until death from any cause. Subjects alive or lost to follow-up at the time of analysis were censored at the date when they were last known to be alive. OS is the most reliable cancer endpoint since it is precise and easy to measure, documented by the date of death, and bias is not a factor in endpoint measurement (FDA, 2020).
L792: Secondary endpoints included EFS, CR rate, percentage of subjects achieving CR with *FLT3*-ITD MRD negativity, composite complete remission (CRc) rate, and rate of subjects achieving CRc with *FLT3*-ITD MRD negativity.
L793: - EFS was defined as the time from randomization until the date of the earliest of any of the following: refractory disease or treatment failure as determined at the end of the Induction Phase (ITF), relapse, or death from any cause. In the clinical study protocol, ITF was defined as failure to achieve CR or CRi by the end of the Induction Phase. The assessment of response was allowed up to Day 56 from the start of the last cycle of induction. The Applicant incorporated the Agency's feedback to define ITF as failure to achieve CR within 42 days of the start of the last cycle of induction chemotherapy for the primary analysis of EFS and the original protocol definition of EFS was maintained as a sensitivity analysis.
- CR and CRc rates were the percentages of subjects achieving CR or CRc after induction, respectively.
- Percentages of subjects achieving CR with *FLT3*-ITD MRD negativity or CRc with *FLT3*-ITD MRD negativity following induction therapy. MRD is the presence of a small number of leukemic cells in the bone marrow or blood of subjects with AML below the level of detection using conventional morphologic assessment. The *FLT3*-ITD MRD assessment by next-generation sequencing was used to detect and quantify residual *FLT3*-ITD mutations present in a fraction of remaining leukemic cells in bone marrow or blood and assessments were made using a cutoff of 1 \times 10^{-4} (1 in 10,000 cells).
L794: Exploratory endpoints considered important to characterize overall efficacy were relapse-free survival (RFS), duration of CR, and cumulative incidence of relapse (CIR).
L795: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 216993}
## {VANFLYTA; quizartinib}
L796: - RFS for subjects who achieved CR or CRi in the Induction Phase was defined as the time from randomization until documented relapse or death from any cause, whichever came first.
- Duration of CR was defined as the time from the first documented CR until the date of documented relapse or death from any cause, whichever came first.
- CIR (post hoc analysis) was defined as the cumulative incidence of relapse from randomization in all subjects who achieved a CR during the Induction Phase.
L797: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L798: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of the study endpoints.
L799: ## **Statistical Analysis Plan (SAP) and Amendments**
L800: ## **The Applicant's Description:**
L801: The primary analysis for OS was conducted after 287 OS events were observed with a minimum of 24 months of follow-up since the time of the last subject randomized. Sensitivity and supplementary analyses to examine the robustness of the OS results were conducted as appropriate. The comparison of the distribution of OS between treatments arms was made using a stratified log-rank test performed at a 2-sided \alpha = 0.05 level with the three stratification factors used for randomization. The median OS was calculated based on Kaplan-Meier estimates and the corresponding 95% confidence interval (CI) was calculated using the method provided by Brookmeyer and Crowley (Brookmeyer, 1982). The hazard ratio (HR) with 95% CI for treatment group was estimated using stratified Cox proportional hazards (PH) models, with the 3 stratification factors used for randomization per IXRS.
L802: EFS was analyzed using the stratified log-rank test and stratified Cox PH model. Subjects who had no EFS events were censored at the last response assessment date. The primary analysis for EFS was based on the response assessment by Independent Review Committee (IRC) with ITF defined as not achieving response of CR, using a 42-day window from the start of the last cycle in induction for CR evaluation. The ITF definition in the original protocol considering CR and CRi by the end of induction (ie, within a 56-day window) was used in the sensitivity and supplementary analysis of EFS. Further details on these analyses are provided in the SAP.
L803: **Analysis sets:** The Intent-to-treat (ITT) Analysis Set included all subjects who were randomized. The Safety Analysis Set included all subjects who received at least one dose of quizartinib/placebo.
L804: **Method for handling missing data:** In general, missing or dropout data were not imputed for the purpose of data analysis, unless otherwise specified. In the case where AE and prior/concomitant medication start dates and/or stop dates were partial or missing, refer to Section 8.2.3 of the SAP for detailed imputation rules (Appendix 16.1.9). The censoring rules
L805: for OS and EFS are detailed in the SAP.
L806: *Statistical methods for multiplicity:* To control for the family-wise type I error rate for primary and secondary efficacy endpoints, a serial hierarchically ordered gatekeeping strategy was employed. The primary assessment of OS in the ITT Analysis Set was evaluated first, and if significant at a 2-sided alpha of 0.05, a statistical evaluation of EFS in the ITT Analysis Set was to be performed. After EFS evaluation, the order of other secondary endpoints to be tested was CR rate, rate of subjects achieving CR with *FLT3*-ITD MRD negativity, CRc rate, and rate of subjects achieving CRc with *FLT3*-ITD MRD negativity. Testing was to be stopped once one test in the sequence failed to be statistically significant. No interim analysis was planned or performed.
L807: *Planned subgroup analyses:* Predefined subgroup analyses for OS based on demographics (age, sex, race, and geographical region) and baseline disease characteristics (baseline ECOG PS, WBC count at the time of diagnosis, choice of anthracycline used during the Induction Phase, AML cytogenetic risk score, *FLT3*-ITD VAF at randomization, and *NPM1* mutation status) were conducted.
L808: The subgroup analyses of OS were conducted in a similar manner as the primary analyses of OS. However, the analyses were performed without stratification and no p-values were reported. Forest plots presenting the HRs and 2-sided 95% CIs by subgroup categories were provided on a single plot.
L809: *SAP amendments:* There was one revision made to the initial SAP as summarized in Table 19. Version 2.0 incorporated cumulative changes made to the clinical study protocol up to and including version 7.0 (see Table 20) and was finalized before the database lock occurred.
L810: **Table 19 – SAP and Amendments**
L811: Version	Date	Key Revisions
1.0	01 Mar 2018	Initial version
2.0	11 Oct 2021	Update following Study Protocol version 7.0
L812: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L813: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of the SAP. Note that EFS was the original primary endpoint for this trial, as discussed in the Protocol Amendments section below. The protocol was updated to utilize OS as an additional primary endpoint on 07 Apr 2020. The protocol was again updated on 28 Oct 2020 to make OS the sole primary endpoint. In addition, FDA conducted sensitivity analyses of OS by HSCT status and other subgroups in order to evaluate the effect of HSCT and/or other factors on OS. Please refer to Section 8.1.2 Study Results.
L814: ## **Protocol Amendments**
L815: ## The Applicant's Description:
L816: The clinical Study Protocol AC220-A-U302 was revised 6 times over the course of study conduct as summarized in Table 20.
L817: **Table 20 – AC220-A-U302 Clinical Study Protocol and Amendments**
L818: Version	Date	Key Revisions
1.0	17 Dec 2015	Initial version
2.0	06 Apr 2017	Minor revision
3.0	20 Nov 2018	Extension of Continuation Phase from 12 to up to 36 cycles
4.0	26 Jun 2019	MRD test and definitions specified.

5.0	07 Apr 2020	Changed primary endpoint from EFS to two primary endpoints of EFS and OS. Analysis set to occur after 410 EFS events and 287 OS events for EFS and OS, respectively. Minimum follow-up for OS defined as 24 months.

Hierarchical testing procedure including EFS was added to control FWER. Updated definition of EFS to be consistent with FDA AML Guidance issued Aug 2020.
7.0	26 May 2021	Changed statistical testing hierarchy and defined IRC assessment to be based on EFS definition consistent with FDA AML Guidance issued Aug 2020.

L836: ## Subject Disposition
L837: ## **Data:**
L838: Overall, 3468 subjects were screened for participation in the study, of whom 2929 subjects were screen failures. Of the screen failures, 2556 subjects were determined to be *FLT3-ITD* (–) per central testing, some of which did not satisfy other eligibility criteria, while 2358 (80.5%) were listed on the CRF as screen failures due to a negative results for *FLT3-ITD* status. In total, 539 subjects comprising the ITT Analysis Set were subsequently randomized into the study (268 subjects in quizartinib arm and 271 subjects in placebo arm). Six subjects (3 in each arm) did not receive any study treatment, hence, the safety population comprised of 533 subjects (265 subjects in quizartinib arm and 268 subjects in placebo arm), all of which entered the Induction Phase.

L849: ## The Applicant's Position:
L850: Similar percentages of subjects in the quizartinib and placebo arm received 2 cycles of induction, discontinued study drug during Induction and Consolidation Phase. However, more subjects entered the Continuation Phase in the quizartinib arm compared to placebo. Also, similar percentages of subjects received protocol-specified HSCT.
L851: While similar percentages of subjects discontinued study drug over all phases, there are notable differences in rates between the quizartinib and placebo arm due to refractory disease or relapse, with fewer such events on the quizartinib arm, while discontinuation of study drug due to AEs was more frequent in the quizartinib arm. Overall, there were a higher percentage of subjects alive and continuing in long-term follow-up and fewer study discontinuations, mainly due to fewer subjects dying, on the quizartinib arm compared to the placebo arm. These differences between arms are reflective of a treatment effect observed for quizartinib.
L852: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L853: FDA agrees with the Applicant's summary of subject disposition as of the data cutoff date of August 13, 2021. We note that the more patients discontinued study treatment due to AE on the quizartinib arm (21.6% quizartinib versus 8.6% placebo). However, discontinuation of study drug due to refractory disease or disease relapse was higher on the placebo arm (32% quizartinib versus 50% placebo). Since discontinuation of study drug due to disease
L854: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 216993}
## {VANFLYTA; quizartinib}
L855: relapsed/refractory disease portends a poor outcome, it is not surprising that more quizartinib patients remained on study at the time of data lock (45% quizartinib versus 38% placebo) and that more patients had discontinued study due to death on the placebo arm (50% quizartinib versus 58% placebo). Lastly, consistent with remaining eligible for later stages of treatment, more quizartinib patients started the continuation phase than placebo treated patients (44% quizartinib versus 34% placebo).

L877: ## The Applicant's Position:
L878: Demographic and baseline characteristics for subjects in the ITT Analysis Set were consistent with a *FLT3*-ITD (+) newly diagnosed AML population. Subjects were evenly distributed among treatment arms by age, sex, race, and ECOG PS.
L879: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L880: FDA agrees with the Applicant's tabulations and assessment of demographic and baseline characteristics for the ITT population. It is notable that eligibility criteria for Study AC220-A-U302 included patients up to age 75. Consequently, 40% of the trial population was \ge 60 years of age, with a full 25% \ge 65 years old. This is an older trial population than was enrolled on the Ratify Study, which enrolled a younger population with an age cutoff of 60. Also of interest, due to the approval of midostaurin by FDA in April 2017, most patients for study AC220-A-U302 were enrolled from Europe and Asia in countries where midostaurin was not available at the time of patient enrollment.

L896: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L897: Relevant baseline characteristics, including AML history and relevant genetic, morphologic, and cytogenetic characteristics were well balanced between arms. The study population had predominantly high-risk features based on cytogenetic risk factors as well as tumor burden, both of which are associated with a worse prognosis. The most frequent FAB type was acute myelomonocytic leukemia, and the majority of subjects in both the quizartinib and placebo arms had an intermediate or unfavorable risk status according to cytogenetic patterns.
L898: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L899: We agree with the Applicant's assessment of the ITT population. The baseline demographics were similar between treatment arms in terms of disease characteristics including cytogenetic risk group, *FLT3*-ITD VAF level, and presenting WBC count. FDA has confirmed the tabulations.
L900: ## **Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use**
L901: ## **Data:**
L902: The median relative dose intensity (ie, the percentage of intended dose administered) was 100% (range: 20%, 207%) in the quizartinib arm and 100% (range: 40%, 200%) in the placebo arm.
L903: All subjects were administered concomitant medications during the study and the use of concomitant medications was generally comparable between subjects in the quizartinib arm and the placebo arm. Major protocol deviations due to the use of prohibited medications were reported in none of the subjects in the quizartinib arm and in 1 (0.4%) subject in the placebo arm.
L904: No rescue medication was used in Study AC220-A-U302.
L905: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L906: Treatment compliance was high and comparable between the quizartinib arm and placebo arm, indicating that quizartinib treatment had comparable tolerability to placebo and concomitant medication use was balanced between the treatment arms.

L940: ## The Applicant's Position:
L941: Study AC220-A-U302 demonstrated a statistically significant and clinically meaningful improvement in OS for quizartinib in combination with chemotherapy then as continuation therapy compared with chemotherapy and placebo followed by placebo as continuation therapy in a population of subjects with newly diagnosed *FLT3*-ITD (+) AML. While Study AC220-A-U302 was not powered to evaluate differences between the prespecified subgroups, the improvement in OS was observed across most of these subgroups in favor of quizartinib.
L942: The improvement in OS is of particular relevance because Study AC220-A-U302 was conducted in a population of subjects with predominantly high-risk features based on cytogenetic risk factors as well as tumor burden, both of which are associated with a worse prognosis and shorter median OS (Levis, 2004) and for whom new and effective therapies represent a particularly high unmet need. The study included younger (\le60 years) and older (&gt;60 years; upper limit was 75 years) subjects notably those who are eligible for intensive therapy, for whom current treatment options remain unsatisfactory.
L943: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L944: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 216993}
## {VANFLYTA; quizartinib}
L945: FDA conducted independent analyses on the primary endpoint OS and found the primary analysis results were consistent with those reported by the Applicant (Table 26 and Figure 2). However, due to plateauing of the survival curves just above and below the median, the median estimates may exaggerate the survival benefit. Furthermore, there was a detrimental effect of OS in the first few months. During Induction, there was a higher number of deaths due to adverse events for quizartinib versus placebo (7.2% vs. 4.5%). The data cut-off was performed when all patients had either discontinued or had at least 24 months of follow-up. Median follow-up time was 39.2 months in both arms. Most of the censoring occurred after 24 months post-randomization.
L946: FDA sensitivity analyses of OS are presented in **Table 28**, including unstratified analysis, stratification factors per eCRF (rather than randomization system as in primary analysis), Per Protocol Set, and censoring OS at the start of conditioning for HSCT. The results of FDA sensitivity analyses of OS from the unstratified analysis and the censoring OS at the start of conditioning for HSCT were comparable to that reported by the Applicant (Table 27). These sensitivity analyses are supportive of the primary analysis result.
