L950: Subgroup analyses were conducted to understand if any patient subgroup was driving the early detriment in mortality. Appendix 19.5.1 presents subgroup results for OS by 3 months, 6 months, or any time. While there is some heterogeneity amongst the subgroups in terms of treatment effect, no single subgroup appears to be driving the early detriment in OS.
L951: Review of the subgroup analysis presented in the Figure 3 Forest Plot above resulted in additional questions about the nature of quizartinib's effect. The following clinical comments are exploratory hypotheses motivated by these results.
L952: *Clinical reviewer comments: In terms of age, we see that while all age groups appeared to benefit from quizartinib treatment, the youngest age group appeared to derive the greatest benefit. It is notable that this age group, patients &lt;60, is analogous to the patient population*
L953: enrolled on the midostaurin pivotal study. However, it is important to remember that age is an independent risk factor for survival in AML, which was again demonstrated with median survival for patients \ge 65 on both arms being inferior to the younger patients.
L954: Another two subgroups that are interesting to consider were based on presenting WBC count and FLT3-ITD VAF level. Again, while the point estimate for all level of these subgroups showed benefit with quizartinib treatment, patients presenting with WBC \ge 40 \times 10^9/L or higher FLT3-ITD VAF level appeared to show greater benefit from quizartinib treatment. This might not be surprising since active FLT3-ITD is thought to drive a particularly proliferative form of AML. Therefore, the higher presenting WBC could indicate greater reliance on the oncogenic signaling from the mutant FLT3-ITD and greater sensitivity to loss of that signal. Along the same line, a higher presenting FLT3-ITD VAF would also argue for greater reliance on FLT3 signaling in the AML clones existing at presentation and sensitivity to loss of that signaling.
L955: Results of the NPM1 subgroup are interesting. NPM1 mutations frequently cooccur with FLT3-ITD mutations and was thought to improve the risk category of the FLT3-ITD AML, at least for FLT3-ITD that has a low VAF. The results above suggest that quizartinib benefits patients that have both FLT3-ITD and NPM1 mutant AML while being neutral for those with FLT3-ITD and WT NPM1. It is not clear why this might be the case. Two things to point out are that the most recent ELN AML guidelines now consider FLT3-ITD with either NPM1 Wt or NPM1 mutant to be intermediate risk disease (Döhner, 2022). The second is that on the control arm, patients with NPM1 mutant disease had a lower median OS than patient with NPM1 Wt disease. Therefore, the result observed on Study AC220-A-U302 regarding NPM1 status likely reflects a combination of 1) recent recognition that in the presence of any level of FLT3-ITD, NPM1 status no longer predicts significant prognostic difference and 2) the relative underperformance of NPM1 mutant/overperformance of NMP1 Wt patients on the placebo arm of the trial. Lastly, it is difficult to make firm conclusions based on this post-hoc subgroup analysis. As noted in Section 6.2.2.2, the results for EFS in the NPM1 Wt subgroup were supportive of positive benefit of quizartinib (HR 0.760).
L956: The last group that suggested a surprising result was response by cytogenetic risk group. While intermediate, unfavorable, and even unknown cytogenetic risk groups appeared to benefit from quizartinib treatment, patients with favorable risk disease appeared to benefit on the placebo arm. Patients in this risk group have either t(15;17), t(8;21), inv(16), or t(16;16). Since patients with t(15;17) were excluded from Study AC220-A-U302, patients in this group were represented by t(8;21), inv(16), or t(16;16) also known as core binding factor AML. This group is considered favorable risk due to its sensitivity to standard treatment and overall better prognosis than other forms of AML. It is unclear why patients that have these cytogenetic rearrangements and FLT3-ITD would benefit more from chemotherapy with placebo than from chemotherapy with
L957: quizartinib. Given the small number of events, this result might be driven by a few chance events and not generalizable to a larger population of such patients.
L958: In addition, FDA conducted the analyses of continuation (i.e., maintenance) treatment effect. Of the 539 randomized patients, 208 patients entered the Continuation Phase (116 [44%] in the quizartinib arm and 92 [34%] in the placebo arm). We define patients who entered the Continuation Phase as the Continuation Analysis Set. In the Continuation Analysis Set, 66/116 (57%) on the quizartinib arm and 49/92 (53%) patients on the placebo arm were treated for &gt;12 cycles of continuation, and 18/116 (16%) and 16/92 (17%) patients, respectively, completed the planned 36 cycles of continuation therapy.
L959: **CDTL Comments:** Of note, the 208/539 (39%) randomized patients who entered the continuation/maintenance phase on QuANTUM-First was greater than the number who entered maintenance on the RATIFY trial of midostaurin (29%).
L960: As shown in Table 29, patients on the quizartinib arm who entered the Continuation Phase were numerically younger and more likely to have WBC \ge 40 \times 10^9/L at baseline.

L970: Below Sections A and B summarized the results of the FDA analyses of the continuation therapy.
L971: ## **Section A: Unadjusted and adjusted analyses of efficacy in the continuation phase**
L972: ## **Unadjusted Analyses**
L973: This section presents crude analyses of efficacy in the continuation phase. These analyses condition on subgroups based on post-randomization events such as entering continuation and receiving HSCT. For simplicity, outcomes are defined from randomization. In general, such
L974: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 216993}
## {VANFLYTA; quizartinib}
L975: analyses are biased, since randomization is broken, and baseline covariates are no longer guaranteed to be balanced. Furthermore, such analyses do not account for differences in time from randomization to continuation or HSCT. Despite potential biases, these analyses are presented to provide a simple overview of efficacy for such subgroups and to provide context for subsequent analyses. Section B below presents analyses which adjust for covariate imbalance and differences in time to continuation or HSCT.
L976: **Figure 4 and Figure 5** present Kaplan-Meier curves of OS and RFS in the 208 patients from the Continuation Analysis Set. As of the data cutoff date (13 August 2021), a total of 51 patients died (25 and 26 in the quizartinib and placebo arm, respectively); median OS was not reached for either arm, and HR = 0.68 (95% CI: 0.4; 1.18) from unstratified Cox model in favor of quizartinib (see Figure 4). The treatment effect appears to plateau around 12 months after randomization.
L977: Similar results are observed in the RFS analysis among 166 patients from the Continuation Analysis Set who achieved CR at the end of Induction. 22 (23%) patients relapsed out of 94 quizartinib patients and 8 (9%) died; 24 (33%) patients relapsed out of 72 placebo patients and 5 (7%) died. Median RFS was only reached in the quizartinib arm at 48.6 months but not reached in the placebo arm, with a HR = 0.74 (95% CI: 0.44; 1.23) from unstratified Cox model in favor of quizartinib. The treatment effect appears to plateau around 12 months after randomization (Figure 5), similar to OS.

L999: Source: FDA analysis
L1000: In summary, in the exploratory subgroup analysis of the 89/208 (43%) of patients who received maintenance therapy with VANFLYTA or placebo following consolidation chemotherapy without HSCT, the OS HR was 0.40 (95% CI: 0.19, 0.84). Of 119/208 (57%) of patients who received maintenance therapy with VANFLYTA or placebo following HSCT, the OS (from randomization) HR was 1.62 (95% CI: 0.62, 4.22).
L1001: ## Adjusted Analyses
L1002: FDA conducted adjusted analyses to potentially account for variability in the timing of post-randomization events by setting the landmark time to the time of HSCT or the time of continuation therapy. For patients who entered continuation, the hazard ratio for OS (from start of continuation) was 0.69 (95% CI: 0.4, 1.20). This is similar to the hazard ratio obtained for OS defined from time of randomization.
L1003: Figure 7 presents a comparison of patients who entered continuation by HSCT status (with vs. without) from the start of continuation therapy. Figure 7 suggests that the survival benefit was mainly observed in patients who entered the continuation phase without HSCT.
L1004: **Figure 7: Kaplan-Meier plot of OS from Continuation by HSCT status (Continuation Analysis Set)**

L1010: Additional adjusted analyses are described in Section 19.5.2. These include the Applicant's analyses (propensity score adjustment) as well as additional FDA analyses (landmark analyses and time-varying Cox model analyses).
L1011: ## Section B: Impact of quizartinib on MRD status in the continuation phase
L1012: FDA sought to determine whether there was evidence of increased activity for quizartinib in the setting of MRD positivity prior to continuation. The protocol specified that MRD was to be captured at the following times: Screening; End of Induction; Consolidation Last Cycle; Post-HSCT; Continuation Cycle 1, Day 1; Continuation Cycle 4, Day 1; and End of Treatment (i.e., completion of 36 cycles of continuation or permanent discontinuation).
L1013: Out of 368 CR + CRI responders, 321, 239 and 162 had at least one MRD assessment in Induction, Consolidation, and Continuation, respectively. Out of 208 patients who entered Continuation, 137 patients (74/116 [64%] quizartinib and 63/92 [69%] placebo) had MRD data available at the initiation of this phase of treatment.
L1014: Based on the MRD cutoff of 0.01%, 13 patients (7 and 6 in the quizartinib and placebo arms, respectively) were MRD positive and 124 patients (67 and 57 in the quizartinib and placebo arms, respectively) were MRD negative at the initiation of Continuation. We define patients in
L1015: the Continuation Analysis Set who were MRD positive (or negative) at the initiation of Continuation as the MRD+ (or MRD-) Continuation Analysis Set.
L1016: Given the small and post-hoc nature of the MRD+ Continuation Analysis Set, statistical inference has limited value. Descriptive analyses are presented in Table 33, where the conversion rate from MRD+ to MRD- during the Continuation Phase is summarized.

L1029: Reviewer's Comment: There is no discernible difference in the treatment effect across the various subgroups in Figure 9 and Figure 10. While Figure 9 suggests a possible difference in the treatment effect of quizartinib on OS for patients who receive HSCT by MRD status, this is not clearly reflected in the subset of patients that received maintenance therapy. The small sample size and post-hoc selection of such subgroups makes inference challenging. However, to the extent that MRD status might be useful in identifying which patients might benefit from maintenance quizartinib therapy, the available data are insufficient to make any such recommendation. As noted below, the MRD data are further limited by the fact that a sizeable percentage of the MRD data was either missing or &gt;90 days prior to HSCT.

L1113: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1114: The primary analysis for the secondary endpoint of EFS was based on a definition of ITF as not achieving a CR within 42 days from the start of the last induction cycle per the FDA AML Guidance (FDA, 2020) and was not statistically significant. Because the primary analysis of EFS was not statistically significant, formal hierarchical testing was not continued and results of other secondary efficacy endpoints are presented descriptively. Of note, in the original protocol, EFS was based on ITF defined as a failure to achieve a CR or a CRI at the end of the Induction Phase, using a window of up to 56 days from the start of the last induction cycle. At the time of the update to the EFS definition, all subjects randomized in the study, including those who were ongoing, had completed the Induction Phase.
L1115: A prespecified sensitivity analysis for EFS was performed using a definition of ITF as failure to achieve CR by the end of induction (ie, within the protocol-specified 56 day window). This analysis provides additional supportive evidence for the efficacy of quizartinib (see Table 37).
L1116: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 216993}
## {VANFLYTA; quizartinib}
L1117: Numerically higher CRc rates were evident in the quizartinib arm compared with the placebo arm and were primarily driven by higher rates of CRI in the quizartinib arm than in the placebo arm. Similar rates of CRc with *FLT3*-ITD MRD negativity at the end of induction were observed between the treatment arms at a 1 \times 10^{-4} cutoff. However, when MRD was assessed as detectable versus not detectable (at a cutoff of 0), differences between quizartinib and placebo were observed, suggesting the use of quizartinib in combination with chemotherapy leads to deeper responses (Table 41).
L1118: While the rates of CR and CR with *FLT3*-ITD MRD negativity at the end of induction were similar between treatment arms (see Table 41), both the median duration of CR and the median RFS were substantially longer in subjects who did achieve a CR during induction on the quizartinib arm when compared to the placebo arm, with a difference of 26.2 months and 25.7 months, respectively. In a post hoc analysis, the CIR in subjects achieving a CR in the Induction Phase also shows a higher percentage of subjects in the placebo arm experiencing relapse at 24 months and 36 months. Taken together, these data indicate that quizartinib delays relapse in subjects who achieve a CR during the Induction Phase, which further supports the clinical benefit of quizartinib in the claimed indication.
L1119: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1120: ## **FDA analyses for EFS, the key secondary endpoint:**
L1121: The primary analysis results of EFS are presented in Table 42 and Figure 19: Kaplan-Meier plot of EFS on QuANTUM-First (ITT Analysis Set). Most EFS events are treatment failure, defined as failure to achieve CR within 42 days of start of the last cycle of induction. There is a numerically higher treatment failure rate in the quizartinib arm (51%) than the placebo arm (48%), causing a bigger drop of the Kaplan-Meier curve in the quizartinib arm at Day 1, and therefore, a crossing hazards pattern due to higher relapse rate in the placebo arm after Day 1. The estimated HR is 0.92 (95% CI: 0.75, 1.11) in favor of quizartinib but the corresponding two-sided p value of 0.2371 does not cross the efficacy boundary of 0.05, which is consistent with that reported by the Applicant (Table 37).

L1128: Source: FDA analysis
L1129: FDA conducted sensitivity analyses of EFS as well. Below Table 43 shows the results of the EFS sensitivity analyses, including Sensitivity analysis 1 where the 42-day window for achievement of CR is removed and Sensitivity analysis 2 where treatment failure is defined as failure to achieve CR or CRi by the end of induction without a 42-day window. In Sensitivity analysis 2, HR estimations are lower because of greater treatment failure rate in the placebo versus quizartinib arm. The corresponding Kaplan-Meier curves (not shown but similar to Figure 14 and Figure 15) do not feature the same crossing pattern as in the primary analysis (Figure 19).
L1130: The differences in EFS analyses are driven by longer times to response observed in the quizartinib arm. The median time to CR was 28 days (range: 15, 105) and 23 days (range: 14, 91) in the quizartinib and placebo arm, respectively. Among CRc responders, the median time to CRc was 26 days (range: 12, 105) and 22 days (range: 14, 91), respectively.

L1139: The clinical adjudication of CR responses agreed with the Applicant's assessment of this endpoint on both arms of Study AC220-A-U302.
L1140: Figure 20 shows the Kaplan-Meier plots of duration of response (both CR and CRi). Duration of CR was defined as the time from the first documented CR until the date of documented relapse or death from any cause, whichever came first. Median duration of CR was 38.6 versus 12.4 months in the quizartinib versus placebo arms, respectively; durability of CRi was similar between two arms. Note that these analyses are based on non-randomized subgroups defined by a post-randomization event (CR or CRi). In general, such analyses are biased, since
L1141: randomization is broken, and baseline covariates are no longer guaranteed to be balanced. Therefore, results from the analyses should be interpreted with caution.

L1145: ## Data:
L1146: The data leading to establishing the dose and dose regimen used in Study AC220-A-U302 are summarized in Section 6.2.2.
L1147: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1148: As with other receptor tyrosine kinase inhibitors, maintaining adequate target coverage is important in achieving clinical efficacy. The dosing regimen was established by considering available clinical efficacy and safety data from prior clinical studies, including ex vivo PD data. The efficacy, safety, and tolerability data from Study AC220-A-U302 confirm that the dosing regimen utilized represents an appropriate balance between safety and efficacy considerations.

L1155: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1156: As discussed under the Section Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints, in subjects achieving a CR in the Induction Phase, the response is maintained for a substantially longer duration compared to placebo as measured by the median duration of CR. The Kaplan-Meier plot of the duration of CR shows sustained separation of the curves for up to 3 years and no notable differences in the demographics and baseline disease characteristics of the responders were observed between the 2 treatment arms.
L1157: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1158: See FDA's Assessment under the section "Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints" above.
L1159: ## **Persistence of Effect**
L1160: ## Data:
L1161: Overall, more subjects in the quizartinib arm than the placebo arm entered the Continuation Phase (Table 21: Subject Disposition in Study AC220-A-U302) and approximately 30% of subjects in the quizartinib arm were treated for more than 1 year (Table 70). Addition of quizartinib to standard chemotherapy resulted in a 16.8-month prolongation of median OS and the survival benefit was maintained over time as demonstrated by higher OS rates for quizartinib compared to placebo at 12, 24, 36, and 48 months (Table 26). Additionally, there was sustained separation in RFS curves (see Figure 16). The duration of CR was approximately 3 times longer in subjects treated with quizartinib compared to placebo with sustained separation of the CR curves out to 3 years (see Figure 17), and lower CIR rates were seen in subjects who achieved CR treated with quizartinib compared with those in the placebo arm (see Figure 18).
L1162: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1163: Quizartinib in combination with standard chemotherapy delays relapse and thereby results in improved OS.
L1164: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1165: FDA was able to obtain similar results for the endpoints OS, DOR, and RFS to that presented above under Data section provided by the Applicant. Note that the analyses of DOR and RFS were exploratory without pre-specified statistical test(s).

L1168: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 216993}
## {VANFLYTA; quizartinib}
L1169: In addition to the clinical endpoints, Study AC220-A-U302 was also designed to collect patient-reported outcomes (PRO) as exploratory endpoints. Assessments were conducted using two PRO scales: European Organization for Research and Treatment of Cancer QLQ-C30 (EORTC QLQ-C30) and EuroQol 5 Dimension 5 Level (EQ-5D-5L). EORTC QLQ-C30 is a self-administered, cancer-specific 30-item questionnaire and incorporates scales for global health status/quality of life (QoL), functional status, symptoms, and perceived financial impact of the disease. EQ-5D-5L is a generic and preference-weighted measure for capturing QoL on the day of the assessment. Among the 539 subjects who were included in the ITT Analysis Set, 509 (94.4%) were included in the PRO Analysis Set (N = 254 for quizartinib and N = 255 for placebo).

L1177: Descriptive analyses were conducted to describe the absolute scores and changes from PRO baseline (Induction Cycle 1 Day 8) for each score on the EORTC QLQ-C30 and EQ-5D-5L scales. A mixed-effects model for repeated measures (MMRM) analysis was performed to summarize the longitudinal changes in each QoL score and systematic differences in changes between the quizartinib and placebo arms. The MMRM analysis included change from PRO baseline as the response variable and QoL score at PRO baseline, time/visit, and a treatment-by-time interaction as covariates. A 10-point change in score was established as minimal clinically important difference (MCID) for EORTC QLQ-C30 scales, which is defined as “the smallest difference in score in the domain of interest which patients perceive as beneficial and which would mandate, in the absence of troublesome side effects and excessive cost, a change in the patient’s disease management” (Deschler, 2010; Dimopoulos, 2013; Roussel, 2020; ). A change of 0.06 was used as the MCID for EQ-5D-5L index scores, and a 7-point MCID was used for visual analog scale (VAS) (McClure, 2017).

L1195: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1196: Study AC220-A-U302 is the first study to explore the impact of quizartinib on QoL for adult  patients with *FLT3*-ITD (+) AML. PRO data were collected for exploratory purposes only and the  study was not designed to formally compare the treatment impact of quizartinib on PRO  measures to that of placebo when combined with standard chemotherapy. A clinically  meaningful improvement in QoL over time was observed in both treatment arms, albeit this  observation may be confounded by potential bias introduced by attrition of subjects in later timepoints. When comparing QoL measures between treatment arms, the differences between  them did not exceed the MCID of 10 points, suggesting no detrimental impact on QoL resulting  from the addition of quizartinib to standard-of-care chemotherapy for newly diagnosed  *FLT3*-ITD (+) AML.
L1197: 195 *Version date: July 2021* *(ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)*
L1198: *Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as "Data" and "The Applicant's Position" are completed by the * *Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA. *
L1199: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 216993}
## {VANFLYTA; quizartinib}
L1200: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1201: FDA agrees with the Applicants assessment that the PRO data above shows an improvement (&gt; 10-point change) for both arms from the baseline measurement. However, we note that this is not necessarily surprising considering that the baseline was assessed in patients recently diagnosed with acute leukemia and after just having completed 7 days of intensive chemotherapy. We also note that later timepoints of analysis occur only in patients that live and remained on study to report their improvement from this QOL nadir at the time of the baseline measurement.
L1202: We also note that during the intensive periods of treatment (induction and consolidation) the QOL measures are almost identical between treatment arms. However, during the continuation phase, there appeared to be some separation across the selected PRO endpoints which numerically favors the placebo arm over the control arm. Of note, the interpretation should be taken with caution.
L1203: *Clinical reviewer comments: This result is perhaps not surprising since the beneficial effect of quizartinib within this study design was to prevent relapse of disease. That is, the postulated benefit of quizartinib is to persist on study and be alive at the cost of added toxicity. This leads us to question the utility of QOL investigation in the setting of remission when the testing is against a placebo. Since one cannot feel better due to the benefit of the drug in this design, what in effect is being assessed is the toxicity burden of the continuation therapy against placebo treatment. If the study was assessing the toxicity burden of two comparative treatments in a continuation setting, QOL measure in addition to standard analysis of toxicity could add to our understanding of overall tolerability.*
L1204: ## **Additional Analyses Conducted on the Individual Trial**
L1205: ## **Data:**
L1206: ## *Analysis of HSCT in Study AC220-A-U302*
L1207: Per protocol in Study AC220-A-U302, subjects were permitted to undergo allo-HSCT after CR or CRi was achieved. Allo-HSCT was to be performed after the Induction Phase, any time during the Consolidation Phase, or, if certain criteria were met, within the first 3 months of the Continuation Phase.

L1216: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1217: The rates of HSCT were high in both treatment arms, which reflects the clinical practice for this subpopulation of high-risk patients with *FLT3*-ITD (+) AML. Rates of HSCT overall and during the first CR (CR1) were numerically higher with quizartinib than with placebo. Approximately 30% of subjects in the quizartinib arm and 27% in the placebo arm were transplanted in CR1, which is considered standard of care for *FLT3*-ITD (+) AML in many countries.
L1218: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1219: The timing of HSCT with respect to disease status was similar on both arms, with most transplantations occurring as part of first line therapy (either during first CR or in patients who did not achieve a CR). Despite the high frequency of HSCT on the study, the OS benefit in favor
L1220: of the quizartinib arm appears consistent when OS is censored at the time of HSCT (Table 27).
L1221: Also, FDA requested the Applicant to clarify the numbers in Table 47, specifically the 157 patients receiving HSCT in first complete remission (CR) on 29 March 2023. From datasets ADRS and ADSL, FDA got 297 subjects achieving CR (only first time CR counted) by INDEPENDENT ASSESSOR, among which 179 HSCT in first CR across the arms, while 245 by INVESTIGATOR, among which 145 HSCT in first CR across the arms. The Applicant responded on 17 April 2023, clarifying that “Two-hundred and ninety-seven (297) subjects achieved CR. Of those, 179 CR patients received HSCT (157 with HSCT in CR1 and 22 with HSCT after relapse).”

L1235: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1236: Although the study was not designed to assess the impact of *NPM1* mutation status on treatment outcomes, the results seen in the mutant *NPM1* (*NPM1*mut) and WT *NPM1* (*NPM1*wt) subgroups are consistent with the EFS and CR/CRc analyses in the overall study population. Consistent with the well-established role of *NPM1*wt as an adverse prognostic factor (Pollyea, 2021; Döhner, 2017), better outcomes were observed in *NPM1*mut subjects compared to *NPM1*wt subjects, including the longer median EFS based on the primary and 2 sensitivity EFS analyses, as well as the higher rates of CR and CRc in *NPM1*mut subjects versus *NPM1*wt subjects.
L1237: No differences were seen when comparing the rates of CR or CRc for quizartinib and placebo in both the *NPM1*mut and *NPM1*wt subgroups, which is consistent with the results seen in the overall population. Similarly, when comparing the treatment effect of quizartinib versus placebo based on EFS in both the *NPM1*mut and *NPM1*wt subgroups the results are consistent with the trends in the overall population. When considering the protocol-defined EFS based on ITF as not achieving CRc by the end of the Induction Phase, HRs of 0.760 in the *NPM1*wt subgroup and 0.696 in the *NPM1*mut subgroup were observed, suggesting there may be a
L1238: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 216993}
## {VANFLYTA; quizartinib}
L1239: benefit of quizartinib in both *NPM1* subgroups. It is important to recognize the high unmet medical need in the high-risk *FLT3*-ITD (+) *NPM1*wt population where treatment outcomes associated with available therapies remain unsatisfactory.
L1240: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1241: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment except a few numbers as follows:
L1242: In Table 48, Sensitivity analysis: ITF definition: Not achieving CRc by the end of the Induction Phase, under *NPM1* wild-type columns, 1) the lower boundary of 95% CI of median EFS for quizartinib arm was 2.83, not 0.03; 2) the median EFS (95% CI) for placebo arm were 2.10 (1.64, 3.25), not 0.03 (0.03, 2.86) based on FDA analyses.
L1243: The effect of *NPM1* mutation status on EFS and CR endpoints showed similar trends to those in the OS analysis above. Specifically, that *NPM1* mutated patients treated with quizartinib had the longest median EFS, at least with the censoring rules used for the EFS sensitivity analysis. This same group, *NPM1* mutated quizartinib-treated, had the longest median OS estimate above.
L1244: *Clinical reviewer comment: Perhaps the most striking finding is derived from a look at the control arm. Despite a large difference in CR rate for NPM1* mutant versus *NPM1* Wt (69.3% mutant vs 36.7% Wt), median OS was inferior in the placebo treated *NPM1* mutated patient than in *NPM1* Wt (Median OS 15.1 months *NPM1* mutated vs 17.3 months *NPM1* Wt). As mentioned above, this result agrees with the recent 2022 ELN risk groups that place all *NPM1* mutant patients with *FLT3*-ITD in the intermediate risk group. This essentially means that within the context of *FLT3*-ITD, *NPM1* status does not improve long term outcomes. Lastly, it also points to at least one genetic subtype of AML where there is a strong disconnect between initial response rate and OS. A similar trend between response and OS has been reported in the relapsed setting for *NPM1* mutant AML.
L1245: ## 8.1.3. Supportive Studies in Newly Diagnosed AML
L1246: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1247: The three Phase 1 studies provide supplemental data for the safety and preliminary efficacy of quizartinib in this indication: 2 studies in combination with chemotherapy in newly diagnosed AML (2689-CL-0005 [N=18] and AC220-A-J102 [N=7]) and 1 study as maintenance monotherapy in patients with AML after HSCT (2689-CL-0011 [N=13]). The efficacy results of these studies are summarized below.
L1248: Study 2689-CL-0005 was a Phase 1, open-label, multiple-dose, dose-escalation study in subjects with newly diagnosed AML (*FLT3*-ITD [+] or *FLT3*-ITD [-]) receiving standard induction and
L1249: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 216993}
## {VANFLYTA; quizartinib}
L1250: consolidation chemotherapy. Induction (1 or 2 cycles) consisted of daunorubicin (60 mg/m2/day) on Days 1, 2, and 3; cytarabine (200 mg/m2/day) on Days 1 to 7; and quizartinib (40 or 60 mg/day) on Days 4 to 10 or 4 to 17. A total of 19 subjects were enrolled (18 treated with quizartinib). Subjects were mostly white (78.9%), female (57.9%), with a median age of 43.0 years (between 22 and 60 years). Overall, 16 subjects (84%) achieved some degree of response, and 9 subjects (47%) achieved CR (2689-CL-0005 clinical study report [CSR]).
L1251: Study AC220-A-J102 was a multicenter, open-label, Phase 1b clinical study of quizartinib administered in combination with chemotherapy in Japanese subjects with newly diagnosed AML. Subjects were enrolled in the study irrespective of *FLT3* mutation status (*FLT3*-ITD [+] or *FLT3*-ITD [-]). Quizartinib was administered at a dose of 20 mg/day at Level 1 and 40 mg/day at Level 2. Dose escalation for a single subject was not allowed. The primary objectives of this study were to evaluate the safety and PK of quizartinib when used with standard induction and consolidation therapy in Japanese subjects with newly diagnosed AML, and to determine the recommended dose of quizartinib for Japanese subjects in subsequent phases of development. This study demonstrated that doses of 20 or 40 mg QD quizartinib in combination with standard chemotherapy are generally well tolerated. A total of 7 subjects were enrolled. Subjects were mostly female (57.1%), with a median age of 62 years (between 34 and 68 years). Overall, the response rate was 85.7% (6 subjects), and 5 subjects (71.4%) achieved CRi (AC220-A-J102 CSR).
L1252: Study 2689-CL-0011 was a Phase 1 study that examined maintenance monotherapy with quizartinib in subjects (aged ≥18 years) with AML in remission after receipt of an allo-HSCT. Subject dose was assigned manually by using a modified rolling 6 design. Two dose levels were tested, given daily in continuous 28-day cycles: dose level 1 at 40 mg and dose level 2 at 60 mg. Dose limiting toxicities (DLTs) were assessed for the first 2 cycles, and subjects were allowed to continue up to a maximum of 24 cycles. Thirteen subjects were enrolled, including 1 subject who had previously received quizartinib as a part of Study 2689-CL-0005. All subjects had received a human leukocyte antigen-matched allo-HSCT (4 related stem cell donors and 9 not related stem cell donors), with a median of 56 days (range: 41 to 70 days) before study enrollment. All subjects were *FLT3*-ITD (+) by local testing at the time of diagnosis. The majority of subjects were male (53.8%), and all were white (100%). The median age was 43.0 years (range: 23 to 61 years). Of the 13 total subjects, 10 (77%) received quizartinib for more than a year and 6 subjects (46%) completed 24 cycles. No maximum tolerated dose (MTD) was identified, but 60-mg QD dose was selected as the highest dose for continuous daily administration based on randomized comparison of daily 30 and 60 mg doses in R/R AML. OS ranged from approximately 13 to 142 weeks, with 9 subjects (69%) surviving ≥50 weeks and 4 subjects (31%) surviving &gt;2 years (104 weeks) (2689-CL-0011 CSR).
L1253: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1254: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. These small pilot studies showed that quizartinib could be tolerated when combined with induction chemotherapy and as remission
L1255: maintenance monotherapy for extended periods of time. Due to the small size, heterogeneous treatments given (variation on dose and timing were given), and single arm nature of these trials, conclusions from these studies are limited to claims of tolerability and pooling of overall safety information.
L1256: ## 8.1.4. Supportive Studies in Relapsed/Refractory AML
L1257: ## 8.1.4.1. Pivotal Study in Relapsed/Refractory *FLT3*-ITD (+) AML – Study AC220-007 (QuANTUM-R)
L1258: ## Trial Design
L1259: ## The Applicant's Description:
L1260: Study AC220-007 was a global, randomized, open-label, active-controlled, Phase 3 study that evaluated the efficacy and safety of quizartinib monotherapy compared with salvage chemotherapy in subjects with *FLT3*-ITD (+) AML who were in first relapse within 6 months of first remission or who were refractory after prior therapy, with or without prior HSCT. The study design from the Screening Visit through the Treatment Period is shown in Figure 25.
L1261: **Figure 25 – Study Design Schematic (Screening to Treatment Period) for Study AC220-007**
L1262: **Notes:** Subjects could continue quizartinib or LoDAC until HSCT, determination by the investigator of no clinical benefit, or unacceptable toxicity.
L1263: Subjects who received MEC or FLAG-IDA were to receive 1 cycle of therapy and could receive a second cycle of the same therapy at the investigator's discretion.
L1264: *FLT3*-ITD mutation status was assessed using a companion diagnostic within 14 days of randomization.
L1265: **Trial location:** This study was conducted at 114 sites across 18 countries in North America, Europe, Australia, and Asia.
L1266: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 216993}
## {VANFLYTA; quizartinib}
L1267: *Treatment assignment:* 367 patients were enrolled and randomized in a 2:1 ratio using a permuted block size of 6 to one of the 2 treatment arms: quizartinib arm (245 patients) or salvage chemotherapy arm (122 patients). Randomization was stratified by prior therapy and response and preselected salvage chemotherapy, without regard to investigative site. The chemotherapy agent and regimen were preselected by investigators prior to randomization.
L1268: *Blinding:* This was an open-label study.
L1269: *Study treatment:* Patients were randomized to receive either quizartinib monotherapy (20- or 30-mg tablets administered orally QD) or salvage chemotherapy (administered subcutaneously [low-dose cytarabine (LoDAC)] or IV [mitoxantrone, etoposide, and intermediate-dose cytarabine (MEC) and fludarabine, cytarabine, and granulocyte-colony stimulating factor (G-CSF) with idarubicin (FLAG-IDA)]).
L1270: For patients randomized to quizartinib, the starting dose was 30 mg/day, unless the patient was receiving concurrent therapy with a strong CYP3A inhibitor, in which case the starting dose was 20 mg/day. For those not taking a strong CYP3A inhibitor, the dose of quizartinib was increased from 30 to 60 mg/day starting on Day 16 (\pm 1 day) if the subject's average QTcF, based on triplicate reading, was \le 450 ms on and before Day 15 (\pm 1 day). For subjects taking a strong CYP3A inhibitor, the dose of quizartinib was increased from 20 to 30 mg/day if they met the above QTcF requirements.
L1271: The investigator preselected the specific salvage chemotherapy regimen before randomization. All salvage chemotherapy was administered during 28-day cycles. The start of cycle 2 (MEC, FLAG-IDA, or LoDAC) and subsequent cycles (LoDAC) could be delayed for up to 14 days to allow for recovery from toxicity. Dose reductions were allowed for toxicity and were documented in the source record. Patients receiving salvage chemotherapy, including those who underwent HSCT, were followed on study and no crossover to investigational study drug was planned or allowed.
L1272: *Duration of treatment:* Total duration of treatment with study drug was until HSCT, lack of clinical benefit, or unacceptable toxicity.
L1273: *Choice of control arm:* At the time Study AC220-007 was implemented, guideline-recommended chemotherapy regimens for R/R AML include intensive ('aggressive') cytotoxic regimens for appropriate patients ('fit patients') or low-dose ('less aggressive') regimens for unfit patients. Intensive combinations include cladribine, cytarabine, and G-CSF with or without mitoxantrone or idarubicin; fludarabine, cytarabine, idarubicin, and G-CSF (FLAG) with or without idarubicin (IDA); MEC; and clofarabine, cytarabine, and G-CSF. Therefore, salvage chemotherapy, which may have included high-intensity chemotherapy (MEC or FLAG-IDA) or low-intensity chemotherapy (LoDAC), was chosen as the active comparator for Study AC220-007.
L1274: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 216993}
## {VANFLYTA; quizartinib}
L1275: **Dose modification/dose discontinuation:** Guidelines for dose modification and treatment discontinuation were the same as those in Study AC220-A-U302 (Sections 6.2.2.1 and 8.1.1). For patients randomized to quizartinib in Study AC220-007 who underwent HSCT, quizartinib was to be discontinued 7 days before initiation of a conditioning regimen. Following HSCT, treatment with quizartinib was allowed by the protocol, provided that patients met protocol-specified criteria that were intended to ensure recovery from the effects of HSCT and were able to tolerate continued quizartinib administration.
L1276: **Concomitant medications:** The prohibited concomitant medications and those that were to be avoided were the same as Study AC220-A-U302 (see Section 8.1.1).
L1277: **Diagnostic criteria:** Subjects were enrolled based on *FLT3*-ITD (+) status as determined centrally using the *FLT3*-ITD CTA for the determination of *FLT3*-ITD mutation status from bone marrow and/or blood samples. This CTA is a molecular assay that employs PCR and capillary electrophoresis technology.
L1278: **Inclusion/exclusion criteria:** The study enrolled subjects with R/R *FLT3*-ITD (+) AML.
L1279: ## **Key inclusion criteria:**
L1280: - Morphologically documented primary AML or AML secondary to myelodysplastic syndrome (MDS), as defined by WHO criteria
- In first relapse (with duration of remission of 6 months or less) or refractory after prior therapy (at least 1 cycle of anthracycline- or mitoxantrone-containing induction chemotherapy), with or without HSCT
- Presence of the *FLT3*-ITD activating mutation in bone marrow or peripheral blood (AR as determined by a central laboratory with a cutoff of \ge 3\% *FLT3*-ITD/total *FLT3*)
- Eligible for preselected salvage chemotherapy, according to the investigator's assessment
- Discontinuation of prior AML treatment before the start of study drug administration
- Total serum bilirubin \le 1.5 \times ULN
- Serum AST and/or ALT \le 2.5 \times ULN
L1281: ## **Key exclusion criteria:**
L1282: - APL
- AML secondary to prior chemotherapy for other neoplasms, except AML secondary to prior MDS
- History of another malignancy, unless the candidate had been disease-free for \ge 5 years
- Persistent, clinically significant &gt;Grade 1 nonhematologic toxicity from prior AML therapy
L1283: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 216993}
## {VANFLYTA; quizartinib}
L1284: - Clinically significant graft-versus-host disease (GVHD) or GVHD requiring initiation of treatment or treatment escalation within 21 days, and/or greater than Grade 1 persistent or clinically significant nonhematologic toxicity related to HSCT
- History of or current CNS involvement with AML
- Prior treatment with quizartinib or participation in a prior quizartinib study
- Prior treatment with a FLT3-targeted therapy including sorafenib or investigational FLT3 inhibitors
- Uncontrolled or significant cardiovascular disease, including the following:QTcF &gt;450 ms (average of triplicate determinations)
- Diagnosed or suspected long interval between the start of the Q wave and the end of the T wave (LQTS), or known family history of LQTS
- History of clinically relevant ventricular arrhythmias, such as ventricular tachycardia, ventricular fibrillation, or TdP
- Myocardial infarction within 6 months prior to screening
- Uncontrolled angina pectoris within 6 months prior to screening
- New York Heart Association Class 3 or 4 CHF
- Uncontrolled hypertension
L1285: *Study assessments:* For subjects receiving quizartinib, bone marrow assessments were performed at screening and on Day 1 of Cycle 2 and Cycle 3. Bone marrow assessments were also performed on Day 1 of each subsequent cycle unless the subject had already achieved CR, complete remission with incomplete platelet recovery (CRp), or CRi. In that event, bone marrow testing was repeated after every 3 subsequent cycles, unless there was evidence of relapse. Bone marrow assessments were also performed at the Early Termination/End-of-treatment Visit, and as clinically indicated. Following HSCT, bone marrow assessments were required on Day 1 of post-HSCT Cycle 1, on Day 1 of Cycle 4 and every 3 cycles subsequently, at the Early Termination/End-of-treatment Visit, and as clinically indicated.
L1286: For subjects receiving salvage chemotherapy, bone marrow assessments were required at screening. For high-intensity chemotherapy (MEC or FLAG-IDA), bone marrow assessments were also required on Day 29 (\pm 14 days) of each cycle of therapy, and at the early termination/End-of-treatment Visit, and as clinically indicated. For LoDAC, bone marrow assessments were also required on Day 1 of Cycle 2 and Day 1 of Cycle 3. If a subject continued to receive LoDAC past Cycle 2, bone marrow assessments were repeated on Day 1 of each subsequent cycle unless the subject had already achieved a CR, CRp, or CRi. In that event, bone marrow testing was repeated after every 3 subsequent cycles, unless there was evidence of relapse. Also for LoDAC, bone marrow samples were required at the Early Termination/End-of-treatment Visit, and as clinically indicated.
L1287: Response to treatment was defined per Sponsor-modified Cheson criteria (Cheson, 2003). CR was defined as &lt;5% bone marrow blasts, no blasts with Auer rods, no persistence of
L1288: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 216993}
## {VANFLYTA; quizartinib}
L1289: extramedullary disease, ANC &gt;1 \times 10^9/L, platelet count \ge 100 \times 10^9/L, and RBC and platelet transfusion independence (defined as 4 weeks without RBC transfusions and 1 week without platelet transfusion). CRp was defined as meeting all criteria for CR except with incomplete platelet recovery (&lt;100 \times 10^9/L). CRi was defined as meeting all criteria for CR except with incomplete hematological recovery with residual neutropenia &lt;1 \times 10^9/L and/or platelet count &lt;100 \times 10^9/L. RBC and platelet transfusion independence was not required for CRi.
L1290: During study treatment (quizartinib or salvage chemotherapy), subjects were monitored regularly for toxicities with ECGs, physical examinations, and laboratory tests (hematology, chemistry and urinalysis) at the visits specified in the study protocol.
L1291: Quizartinib dose modifications were required if the subject experienced QTcF prolongation, other nonhematologic toxicities, or myelosuppression, or were receiving concomitant strong CYP3A inhibitors.
L1292: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1293: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment of the study design. We note the following specific components of the salvage chemotherapy arm.
L1294: - LDAC: 20 mg subcutaneously twice daily on days 1-10 of each 28-day cycle; treatment could continue until PD or unacceptable toxicity
- Intensive chemotherapy:MEC:Mitoxantrone (8 mg/m^2/day) by intravenous (IV) on days 1-5
- Etoposide (100 mg/m^2/day) by IV infusion immediately after mitoxantrone on days 1-5
- Cytarabine (1000 mg/m^2/day) by IV infusion immediately after etoposide on days 1-5
- FLAG-IDA:G-CSF (300 \mug/m^2/day) by IV infusion OR (5 \mug/kg/day) on days 1-5Additional GCSF recommended 7 days post completion of chemotherapy, until recovery of ANC to &gt;0.5 \times 10^9/L
- Fludarabine (30 mg/m^2/day) by IV infusion on days 2 – 6
- Cytarabine (2000 mg/m^2/day) by IV infusion, beginning 4 hours after the fludarabine infusion, on days 2 – 6
- Idarubicin (10 mg/m^2/day) IV on days 2-4
L1295: Up to 2 cycles of induction could be given to patients receiving MEC or FLAG-IDA.
L1296: ## **Study Endpoints**
L1297: ## **The Applicant's Description:**
L1298: The primary efficacy endpoint was OS, defined as the time between the date of randomization and the date of death from any cause. The secondary efficacy endpoint was EFS, defined as the time from randomization until documented refractory disease, relapse after CRc, or death from any cause, whichever occurred first.
L1299: Exploratory efficacy endpoints included CRc rate, CR rate, duration of CRc, duration of CR, and transplant rate.
L1300: - CRc rate, which is the percentage of subjects achieving a best response of CR, CRp, or CRi
- CR rate, which is the percentage of subjects achieving a best response of CR
- Duration of CRc and CR, which is the time from the first documented response until the date of documented relapse
- Transplant rate (bridge to transplant), which is the percentage of subjects undergoing allo-HSCT directly following protocol-specified treatment with no intervening AML therapy
L1301: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1302: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of the study endpoints and notes that the protocol did not specifically define induction failure in the definition of EFS.
L1303: ## **Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments**
L1304: ## **The Applicant's Description:**
L1305: All efficacy analyses were performed according to treatment assigned at randomization. All safety analyses were based on actual treatment received. The ITT Analysis Set included all subjects who were randomized. The Safety Analysis Set included all subjects who received at least 1 dose of the study drug. The Per-protocol Set (PPS) included all subjects in the ITT Analysis Set who had no major protocol deviations that would affect assessment of efficacy endpoints. Subjects who were randomized but did not receive any dose of study drug and those who had negative FLT3-ITD test results based on central testing were excluded from the PPS.
L1306: The ITT Analysis Set was used for the analysis of all efficacy endpoints. Supporting analyses were also conducted with the PPS. There were 2 planned cutoff dates for efficacy analyses: 1 interim analysis, when approximately 50% (140 of 280) of the expected number of events (deaths) had occurred, and a final analysis at 280 OS events. EFS was to be analyzed only if the OS result was positive. The actual interim analysis was performed when 153 (55%) OS events were reported. The interim stopping boundary for OS was not crossed at that time, and the data monitoring committee recommended for the study to continue without modification.
L1307: Calculation of sample size was based on comparison of OS, in the 2 treatment groups (quizartinib and salvage chemotherapy) at a 2-sided significance level of 0.05, assuming that
L1308: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 216993}
## {VANFLYTA; quizartinib}
L1309: median survival is 3.9 months in the salvage chemotherapy arm with an increase to 6 months, in the quizartinib arm (HR 0.65). As of the defined data cutoff of 22 Feb 2018, there were 276 deaths to support the primary ITT analysis.
L1310: Analysis of OS and EFS were conducted for the prespecified subgroups including: age; sex; race; geographic region; stratification factors from randomization (prior therapy and response and preselected salvage chemotherapy); *FLT3*-ITD AR at randomization; AML history; prior allo-HSCT; AML cytogenetic risk score; and baseline blast count.
L1311: Safety and tolerability were assessed by incidence, severity, and changes from baseline of all relevant parameters including TEAEs, clinical laboratory values, vital signs, and ECG parameters. Unless otherwise specified, all safety analyses were performed on the Safety Analysis Set and grouped by actual treatment received. AEs to be included were restricted to TEAEs. Death, other serious AEs (SAEs), and AEs associated with dose reduction, dose interruption, and premature discontinuation of study drug were summarized by treatment arm. AEs of special interest (AESIs) in this study were TdP/QT prolongation, hepatic disorders, cardiac failure, cardiac arrhythmias; and sequelae of cytopenias, such as infections and bleeding.
L1312: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1313: We agree with the Applicant's assessment of the SAP. The primary efficacy analyses were to be performed on the intent-to-treat (ITT) population, consisting of all patients randomized. For OS, patients were not censored at the time of subsequent non-protocol AML therapy, including HSCT.
L1314: The study planned to accrue 363 patients and assumed a 10% drop out rate. With 280 deaths and assuming a median OS of 3.9 months in the control arm and 6 months in the quizartinib arm, the study had 90% power and a 2-sided significance level \alpha of 0.05 to detect a hazard ratio of 0.65 in OS. The primary analysis was to take place when 280 events occurred.
L1315: The primary efficacy endpoint OS was to be analyzed by the stratified log-rank test. The stratification factors were to be those used for randomization. A non-stratified log-rank test was to be used as supportive evidence of efficacy.
L1316: The key secondary endpoint EFS was to be analyzed by the stratified log-rank test with covariates to control for response to first-line AML therapy and pre-selected salvage chemotherapy in the ITT. To maintain the overall type 1 error rate at the 0.05 significance level, hypothesis testing in the secondary analysis was to be performed only if the null hypothesis in the primary analysis was rejected at the overall two-sided 0.05 significance level.
L1317: Sensitivity analyses for OS were to include an analysis where patients were censored at the date of HSCT, and an analysis of OS on the PPS, of all patients randomized and without major protocol deviations.
L1318: Sensitivity analyses for EFS were to include an analysis on the PPS.
L1319: One interim analysis was planned at 140 events with an O'Brien-Fleming boundary for superior efficacy and a conditional power (CP) of 10% for futility. The Lan-DeMets spending function was to be employed to control the overall Type I error of 1-sided 0.025 for the primary efficacy analysis of OS such that the nominal Type I error was to be 0.001525 at interim analysis and 0.0245 at the final analysis. For the secondary endpoint EFS, the analysis plan employed a hierarchical ("gatekeeping") testing procedure to ensure a study-wise 1-sided Type 1 error rate of 0.025, such that EFS would be analyzed using the same boundaries and nominal Type 1 error for the primary analysis at the interim or final analysis.

L1321: ## The Applicant's Description:
L1322: The clinical Study Protocol AC220-007 was revised 7 times over the course of study conduct as summarized in Table 50.

L1326: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1327: The first patient was randomized to the study on 5/7/2014, after the first Amendment of the study was dated 12/24/2013. The data cut-off for efficacy for this NDA was 12/21/2020.
L1328: FDA notes that Amendment dated 6/3/2015 clarified timing for quizartinib, changed the dose escalation schedule and requirement of ECG monitoring post dose escalation, changed response assessment timing to allow for count recovery, and revised inclusion criteria to include patients in first relapse with DOR &lt;6 months, including 6 months post-HSCT, and added exclusion criteria for bradycardia due to QTc prolongation. Revisions to the eligibility criteria in Amendment 2 might have contributed to some inconsistencies in prior therapies/relapse data.
L1329: Amendment 5 dated 9/2/2016 increased the planned number of subjects to 363 to reach planned number of events (280) in a "reasonable time," removed references to adaptive design that would allow for increase of sample size at the time of the interim analysis, added efficacy objectives/variables (CR, CRc, duration of CR and CRc), revised dose modifications for patients with QTcF prolongation if already at a reduced dose, added language to help minimize the number of subjects without survival information, revised censoring analysis for HSCT for the sensitivity analysis of OS, and added subgroup analyses for primary and secondary endpoints, and safety analyses. The revision in Amendment 5 with regard to follow-up raises the possibility that the potential for a high rate of early censoring was foreseen already then.
L1330: ## 8.1.4.2. Study Results – Study AC220-007
L1331: ## Subject Disposition
L1332: ## Data and The Applicant's Position:
L1333: Overall, a total of 367 subjects were randomized (245 subjects in quizartinib arm and 122 subjects in salvage chemotherapy arm) and included in the ITT Analysis Set. Details of the subject disposition and treatment and study discontinuation are presented below.
L1334: Figure 26 – Subject Disposition in Study AC220-007

L1341: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 216993}
## {VANFLYTA; quizartinib}
L1342: Sources: AC220-007 CSR Table 14.1.1.1, Table 14.1.1.2, Table 14.1.4.1, Table 14.2.1.6a, Table 14.2.4.1b, Table 14.2.4.1c, Listing 16.2.1.2, Listing 16.2.1.3, and Listing 16.2.6.1.1
L1343: Most subjects randomized to the salvage chemotherapy arm were preselected by the investigator to receive intensive chemotherapy (FLAG-IDA regimen or MEC).
L1344: Of the subjects randomized, 241 and 94 subjects received treatment with quizartinib or salvage chemotherapy, respectively. As a result there was an imbalance in the randomized not treated subjects with a larger number occurring in the salvage chemotherapy arm compared with the quizartinib arm (1.6% vs 23%, respectively).
L1345: A total of 93 subjects had an HSCT immediately following conclusion of study drug without intervening AML therapy. A higher proportion of subjects in the quizartinib arm received HSCTs (79 [32.2%]) than in the salvage chemotherapy arm (14 [11.5%]). At the time of the data cutoff, 21 subjects continued on quizartinib therapy: 6 subjects (2.4%) continued initial quizartinib without intervening HSCT and 15 (6.1%) continued on quizartinib after HSCT. All subjects in the salvage chemotherapy arm discontinued study drug and 16 (13.1%) remained in the study at the time of data cutoff.
L1346: Differences between the treatment arms in the reasons for study drug discontinuation were consistent with the treatment regimens and the longer duration of study drug for subjects on the quizartinib arm compared to the salvage chemotherapy arm. Progressive disease was the most common reason for study drug discontinuation in the salvage chemotherapy arm (40.2%) and was more frequent than in the quizartinib arm (19.2%). The most common reason for study drug discontinuation in the quizartinib arm (initial quizartinib treatment) was HSCT (32.2%).
L1347: Of the 79 subjects randomized to quizartinib who received HSCT immediately following conclusion of study drug without intervening AML therapy, 49 restarted quizartinib after HSCT. Of these 49 subjects, 34 subsequently discontinued quizartinib. The primary reasons for discontinuing quizartinib post-HSCT were relapse (17 subjects) or AEs (10 subjects).
L1348: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1349: For the purposes of tabulation of early discontinuations in Table 51, the term "lack of efficacy" included events including relapse, progression, lack of efficacy, or an AE with a PT related to the underlying disease or relapse.

L1354: Notably, there was an imbalance in the number of patients randomized but not treated, consisting of only 4 patients (2%) on the quizartinib arm compared to 28 patients (23%) on the salvage arm. Further, while all 4 patients on the quizartinib continued to be followed on study, and reason for discontinuation was death, of the 28 patients in the salvage arm, 17 withdrew from the study, including 15 in whom this happened within a week of randomization, 1 in which this happened within 10 days, and one in which this happened on day 36. With the exception of 2 patients for whom a death date was available, the remaining 15 patients were censored on the day of study discontinuation with the reason "no survival follow-up within 3 months."
L1355: Reviewer comment: The imbalance noted above, both in terms of the patients randomized and not treated and to the early censoring for the primary endpoint that occurred almost exclusively on the control arm, introduces potential bias that raises concerns regarding the credibility of the study results. It is possible that the OS for the 15 patients censored early would improve the results for the salvage arm to an extent that the difference between the arms, which is quite small (6.5 weeks), would be rendered statistically insignificant. FDA also noted an imbalance in early censoring between treatment arms with 9 patients on the control arm censored prior to
L1356: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 216993}
## {VANFLYTA; quizartinib}
L1357: week 8 and an additional patient on that arm was censored at exactly week 8 (day 56), while only 1 patient on the quizartinib arm was censored within the first 8 weeks. Various sensitivity analyses need to be done to assess for robustness of the study results.

L1364: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1365: Demographic and baseline characteristics for subjects in the ITT Analysis Set were consistent with a R/R FLT3-ITD (+) AML population and similar between treatment arms.
L1366: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1367: Demographic information for the patients on Study AC220-007 are summarized in Table 52. The median age was slightly younger in the quizartinib arm, but the range was wider, including a patient who was 81 years old. The majority of the patients were females on the quizartinib arm and males on the control arm. Approximately one-third of the patients on each arm were treated in the US. The slightly higher percentage of patients on the salvage arm with an ECOG PS of 2 may bias the study results in favor of the quizartinib arm.

L1382: The higher rate of unknown cytogenetics for the salvage arm raises concerns regarding an imbalance in expected outcomes between the arms. L1388: As expected, the median age in the low intensity stratum was higher than in those stratified to high intensity therapy, as was the rate of patients over 65 in that strata (50% of patients in each arm among those pre-selected for low intensity therapy compared to approximately 20% for those pre-selected for high intensity therapy). The striking imbalance between patients with an
L1389: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 216993}
## {VANFLYTA; quizartinib}
L1390: ECOG PS of 2 between the arms in the low intensity stratify highlights the difference in the ITT population, but this is the stratum more affected by the imbalance, and may bias the study results in favor of the quizartinib treated arm. The fact that 23% of patients in the low intensity stratum had prior HSCT while none in the control arm in this stratum is also concerning for imbalance, but this can be in both directions: if it is due to the fact that the patients disease was responsive to therapy and thus allowed for achievement of CR that allowed for transplant, while patients in the salvage arm had more resistant disease, this would bias the results in favor of quizartinib; in contrast, the morbidities due to prior transplant (that might be the reason for pre-selection to this stratum by the investigators) may increase the risk of toxicities in the quizartinib arm and bias the results in favor of the control arm. Since these cannot be determined, uncertainty remains between this imbalance, and must be taken into account upon interpretation of the study results. The imbalance in cytogenetic risk in the low risk stratum (specifically unfavorable risk) also biases the results in favor of the quizartinib arm; however, since the cytogenetic risk stratification system used for definitions of these categories is fairly outdated, the impact on the study results is uncertain.

L1421: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1422: In Study AC220-007, treatment with quizartinib monotherapy resulted in a statistically significant and clinically meaningful improvement in OS compared with salvage chemotherapy (1-sided P = 0.0177 by stratified log-rank test) in a population of subjects with R/R *FLT3*-ITD (+) AML. The improvement in OS was supported by 3 preplanned and several post hoc sensitivity analyses, all of which showed treatment effects that were consistent with that of the overall ITT analysis. Despite questions raised by the FDA over the robustness of the OS data, the results nevertheless are supportive of clinical benefit of quizartinib monotherapy in *FLT3*-ITD (+) AML patients. The improvement in OS was also observed across most prespecified subgroups in favor of quizartinib, which is supportive of efficacy in the overall population enrolled in Study AC220-007.
L1423: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1424: As mentioned above (disposition) the high rate of early censoring, highly imbalanced between the arms, and the relatively small difference between the median OS in each arm, raised concerns regarding the credibility of the study results for the primary endpoint. If the OS of the ITT population on the salvage arm was even slightly better than the results with this censoring, the HR and p-value might not hold up. In addition, the 6.5-week survival advantage may not be clinically meaningful given safety concerns. Thus, the benefits were previously determined by
L1425: NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 216993}
{VANFLYTA; quizartinib}
L1426: FDA to not outweigh the risks as monotherapy in patients with R/R FLT3-ITD mutated AML. However, the OS results for quizartinib in comparison to salvage chemotherapy provide some support for the clinical activity of quizartinib in patients with FLT3-ITD mutated AML.
L1427: See also the FDA Briefing Document, 2019 for comprehensive analyses conducted by the review team at the time of the initial NDA review for quizartinib.
L1428: ## Data Quality and Integrity
L1429: ## Data:
L1430: There were 28 subjects whose last survival assessment was more than 3 months prior to the cutoff date for the study-wide, final analysis data collection. These included the subjects who were censored at less than 8 weeks (n = 18), plus an additional 10 subjects. At the FDA's request, the Applicant conducted a targeted and limited public record sweep and found data on 13 of these subjects. An updated ITT analysis including these subjects was consistent with the original ITT analysis. The median OS was 26.9 (95% CI 23.1-31.0) weeks for the quizartinib arm compared with 20.4 (95% CI 17.0-25.3) weeks for the salvage chemotherapy arm (HR 0.77 [95% CI 0.60, 0.99]; P = 0.0175).
L1431: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1432: The updated ITT analysis was consistent with the original ITT analysis for OS for AC220-007 and supportive of use of quizartinib in combination with standard chemotherapy in the treatment of patients with newly diagnosed *FLT3-ITD* (+) AML.
L1433: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1434: Based on the updated data received, there were still 6 patients, all on the control arm, for whom censoring occurred within 16 days of randomization without any further information. An additional patient, on the quizartinib arm, was censored at exactly 6 weeks, 3 other patients on the control arm were censored between 6 and 8 weeks, and 1 patient on the control arm was censored at exactly 8 weeks. When looking at all censored patients for whom "no survival follow-up" is the reason for censoring (beyond 8 weeks), 4 patients remain, 2 on each arm. While the updated data was somewhat helpful in decreasing the amount of uncertainty with regard to the outcome for the primary endpoint, the imbalance in early censoring (9 patients on the control arm and 1 patient on the quizartinib arm censored before 8 weeks, with the all censoring within the first 16 days being on the control arm) left residual uncertainty with regard to the credibility of the study results.

L1457: b Kaplan-Meier estimate. CI for median was computed using the Brookmeyer-Crowley method. The denominator for percentages is the number of subjects with CRc.
L1458: c Transplant rate if the percentage of subjects undergoing allo-HSCT directly following the protocol treatment with no intervening AML therapy. Rate and CI are calculated based on binomial distribution.
L1459: Notes: The denominator for percentages is number of subjects in ITT Analysis Set unless otherwise specified.
L1460: Source: AC220-007 CSR Tables 14.2.3.1, 14.2.4.1, and 14.2.6.1
L1461: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1462: The secondary endpoint of EFS showed quizartinib was not statistically significantly different from salvage chemotherapy but did demonstrate a trend in favor of the quizartinib arm. A number of exploratory endpoints including CR rate, CRc rate, duration of CRc, and transplant rate numerically favored the quizartinib arm.
L1463: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1464: One of the exploratory endpoints of the trial was CR rate, and duration of CR as well as composite complete remission (CRc) rate, as defined above. FDA considers durable CR a clinical benefit outcome in patients with relapsed/refractory AML (Jen, 2018); durable CRh (defined as
L1465: &lt;5% bone marrow blasts by morphological examination with an ANC of &gt;0.5 Gi/L and a platelet count of &gt;50Gi/L, that do not meet CR requirements, in the absence of peripheral blasts or extramedullary disease) may be considered a clinical benefit endpoint in a population of patients being treated without curative intent, using agents that are relatively non-toxic and not myelosuppressive; even if achieved, it may require supporting data for transfusion independence in order to be considered a direct clinical benefit. However, CRI is not considered an endpoint equivalent to CR for regulatory decision making.
L1466: In the FDA analysis of remission rates on Study 007, FDA adjudicated each CR or CRh based on the datasets including bone marrow blast percentage and accompanying ANC and platelet counts. Since per the protocol and SAP, the CR requirements above had to be met in the absence of a RBC transfusion in the prior 4 weeks and platelet transfusion in the prior 1 week, FDA performed the per protocol analysis, but also performed a sensitivity analysis where CRs and CRhs were adjudicated solely on the basis of the bone marrow aspirate, CBC and extramedullary disease results described above. The protocol did not specify the timeframe within which the CBC results for determination of CR or CRh had to be collected in relation to the timing of the bone marrow assessment; for FDA adjudication of these outcomes, a 7-day window was used; a sensitivity analysis allowing for a window of 14 days was formed by FDA as well. The results of the applicant's analysis, as well as that of the two FDA analyses, are summarized in below; the specific patients for whom the FDA adjudicated CR or CRh rates differed from those assessed by the applicant in the sensitivity analyses are listed in the text below the table.
L1467: **Table 61: CR and CRh rates, Study AC220-007**

L1470: To look for overall efficacy signals, FDA performed a final sensitivity analysis in which all patients with &lt;5% blasts in their BM were considered CR if they met the peripheral count recovery criteria at any point (even beyond 14 days) after the BM assessment, and CRh if they met those criteria at any point. Using these criteria, there was no change in the results for the quizartinib arm (14 CRs and 13 CRhs), but for the control arm, there were an additional 2 CRs for a total of 6 CRs (5%) and 2 CRhs (2%), for a CR+CRh rate of 7% (8/122). L1475: The results of the CR and CRh analyses above raised concerns regarding the benefit of quizartinib in the population proposed in the initial NDA review. Since CR, and CR+ CRh in certain clinical settings, are endpoints representative of clinical benefit that have been used as the basis of approval of agents for the treatment of AML, a finding of high CR and/or CR+CRh rates in the quizartinib arm on Study AC220-007 would have supported the OS findings above and served as further evidence of efficacy in this population; conversely, the low CR and CRh rates described above raised further doubt regarding the results of the primary endpoint, as they were not internally consistent or robust. However, considering the results of QuANTUM-First described above, the rate of CR+CRh responses with quizartinib is reflective of measurable monotherapy activity of quizartinib in patients with FLT3-ITD mutated AML.
L1476: When CR/CRh rates have been used as the basis for marketing approval in the treatment of AML (e.g. ivosidenib, enasidenib, gilteritinib), supportive evidence for transfusion independence (TI) is necessary to strengthen the evidence of clinical benefit that CR/CRh represents (i.e., the recovery of counts to the point where transfusions are not necessary). On Study AC220-007, FDA performed an analysis of patients on the quizartinib arm who were transfusion dependent (TD) at study entry and achieved a 56-day window of transfusion independence at some point post-baseline. The denominator included 99 patients on the quizartinib arm who had documented transfusions through study day -1. In this analysis, 26 of these patients (26%; 95% CI: 19, 36) achieved TI at some point on treatment. In the context of the original NDA review, in the absence of evidence of substantial effectiveness of the drug on the underlying disease (i.e., CR and/or CR/CRh), TI alone could not be used as the sole evidence of benefit for quizartinib in patients with R/R FLT3-ITD mutated AML. However, we consider the data on TI from AC220-007 to be supportive of some monotherapy activity of quizartinib.
L1477: ## Dose/Dose Response
L1478: ## Data:
L1479: The data leading to establishing the dose and dose regimen used in Study AC220-007 are summarized in Section 6.2.2.1.
L1480: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1481: The efficacy, safety, and tolerability data from Study AC220-007 confirm that the dosing regimen utilized represents an appropriate balance between safety and efficacy considerations and supports the use of quizartinib in combination with standard chemotherapy for patients with *FLT3*-ITD (+) AML.
L1482: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1483: On Study AC220-007, patients on the quizartinib arm were treated with different starting doses depending on whether they were on concomitant CYP3A4 inhibitor (20 mg) or not (30 mg), and dose escalation on day 16 was dependent on this factor (to 30 mg or 60 mg) as well as the QTc value on day 15, such that a dose- and exposure- response analysis would be appropriate. However, given the issues with regard to reliability of the study results as pertaining to the primary endpoint, interpretability of these analyses are subject to the same limitations as those described above with regard to interpretation of the overall study results, and are not further described here.
L1484: Due to the small numbers of CR/CRhs on Study AC220-007, response cannot be used as a clinical benefit endpoint that would allow for these analyses by dose either.
L1485: ## **Durability of Response**
L1486: ## **Data:**
L1487: See Table 60.
L1488: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1489: Overall, the responses are durable with observation of longer median duration of remission for CRc and CR.
L1490: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1491: An analysis of durability of response was not performed due to the very small numbers of patients on AC220-007 who achieved a CR/CRh, such that these would not be used as substantial evidence of effectiveness in this context.

L1495: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1496: The persistence of effect of quizartinib was established by the results of Study AC220-007. For the primary analysis of OS (ITT Analysis Set), the Kaplan-Meier plot of OS demonstrated early
L1497: and clear separation between the curves that was sustained thereafter (Figure 27). At 52 weeks (1 year), the survival probability was 27% of subjects for quizartinib compared with 20% for salvage chemotherapy.
L1498: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
