Total error	Not analyzed
Selectivity & matrix effect	Selectivity was tested using 6 lots of blank matrix. No interference observed in the matrix at the retention window of either analytes or internal standards.	
Matrix effect was tested using 6 lots of blank matrix extraction spiked with analytes at LQC and HQC against neat spikes. Internal standard normalized matrix factors are: Quizartinib: 0.99 to 1.02 with CV% ranged from 1.3 to 1.5%. AC886: 0.965 to 0.991 with CV% ranged from 1.4 to 3.4 %.
No matrix effect observed.
L2430: Interference & specificity	
Interference & specificity were evaluated in blank matrix, LQC and HQC spiked with the interfering drugs (and some of their metabolites) and common OTCs. The blank matrix samples were measured to be below LLOQ. For spiked QC responses:
Quizartinib: Bias% ranged from -8.3 to 2.7% with CV%\le2.9%.
AC886: Bias% ranged from -4.0 to -2.0% with CV%\le4.3.
No impact from co-administered drugs or common OTCs to the quantitation of either analyte (furosemide, lansoprazole, propranolol, spironolactone and rifaximin).

Hemolysis effecta	
Hemolytic effect was tested at LQC and HQC using plasma with 2% (v/v) of lysed blood.
Quizartinib: Bias% ranged from -4.0 to -3.3% with CV%\le8.3%
AC886: Bias% ranged from -2.3 to 0.7% with CV%\le3.4%.
No hemolysis effect observed.

Lipemic effecta	
Lipemic effect was tested with 6 lots of blank hyperlipidemic (2% lipid) matrix and spiked with LLOQ level. The responses from blank hyperlipidemic matrix relative to LLOQ were measured to be below 5% for quizartinib and below 4% for AC886.
For 6 different lipidemic blank matrix samples spiked at LLOQ level:
Quizartinib: Mean response Bias% is 0.8% with CV% \le3.9%. AC886: Mean response Bias% is 5.8% with CV%\le5.7%.
No lipemic effect observed.

Dilution linearity & hook effect	
Dilution linearity was established at 1500 and 5000 ng/mL (n = 6) using a factor of 20. Back-calculated mean values of 6 replicates against nominal for:
Quizartinib: Bias% ranged from -3.3% to 2.6% with %CV \le3.5% AC886: Bias% ranged from -6.0% to 1.0 % with %CV \le2.6%
The hook effect evaluate is not applicable for LC/MS/MS assay.

Bench-top/process stability	
Bench-top stability (short term) was established by using LQC and HQC for 24 hours at RT.
Quizartinib: %Bias from -3.3% to -2.8% with %CV \le5.3%
AC886 %Bias from -2.0% to 5.3% with %CV \le4.7%
Process stability was established by using LQC and HQC for 84 hours at 2 to 8°C
Quizartinib: %Bias from -2.7% to 0.0% with %CV \le3.0%
AC886 %Bias from -1.3% to 7.3% with %CV\le8.0%

Freeze-Thaw stability	
5 freeze-thaw cycles at -20°C and -80°C were established for both analytes at LQC (1.50 ng/mL) and HQC (400 ng/mL).
5 freeze-thaw cycles	@-20°C	@-80°C
%Bias	%CV	%Bias	%CV
Quizartinib	LQC	0.7	\le4.5	-11.3	\le6.6
HQC	-0.8	-9.8	
AC886	LQC	-4.0	\le3.8	-2.7	\le3.2
HQC	-4.5	-6.5
L2431: Long-term stabilityb	Quizartinib was established by using LQC and HQC for:
409 days at -20°C: %Bias from -8.0% to 1.3% with CV% \le5.5%
728 days at -80°C: %Bias from 6.5% to 12.9% with CV% \le8.8%
AC886 was established by using LQC and HQC for:
959 days at -20°C: %Bias from -7.3% to 12.7% with CV% \le6.9%
727 days at -80°C: %Bias from 7.8% to 14.7% with CV% \le3.9%
Parallelism	Not applicable
Carry over	A blank plasma sample (without IS) was injected immediately after ULOQ sample. Small percentage of carryover was observed with no impact data quality.
Clinical Studies	AC220-016, AC220-018, AC220-019, AC220-A-J101, AC220-A-J102, AC220-A-J201, AC220-A-U104, AC220-A-U105, AC220-A-U106, AC220-A-U107, AC220-A-U302
L2432: a Lipemic and additional long-term stability data are summarized in Report 1000-141851-1 Addendum 2.
L2433: b Long-term stability data are summarized in Report 1000-141851-1 Addendum 1.

L2435: ## 19.4.2. Population PK Analysis
L2436: ## 19.4.1.1 Executive Summary
L2437: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2438: The proposed induction and consolidation dosage and dose adjustments for concomitant strong CYP3A4 inhibitor administration are supported by the population PK (PPK) analysis.
L2439: Exposure is expected to be significantly higher following the proposed dosage during maintenance compared to the proposed dosage during induction and consolidation. The acceptability of exposure during maintenance treatment warranted further assessment detailed in Section 19.4.3.2. and additional data is required to determine an appropriate maintenance dose as described by the PMR in Section 6.2.2.3.
L2440: PPK analysis did not identify any clinically relevant differences in quizartinib or AC886 exposure according to age, weight, sex, race, weak or moderate CYP3A4 inhibitors, mild or moderate renal impairment, and mild or moderate hepatic impairment. The effect of severe renal impairment and severe hepatic impairment on quizartinib and AC886 PK has not been studied.
L2441: ## **19.4.1.2 PPK Assessment Summary**
L2442: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2443: General Information
Objectives of PPK Analysis	Characterize quizartinib and its active metabolite AC886 PK profiles
Identify the source of PK variability to support individualized dosing
Predict individual exposure for E-R assessment in Study AC220-A-U302

Study Included	AC220-014, AC220-015, AC220-016, AC220-018, AC220-A-U105, 2689-CL-0005, AC220-A-J102, 2689-CL-2004, 2689-CL-0011, AC220-A-J101, AC220-A-J201, AC220-007 and AC220-A-U302
Dose(s) Included	20 mg, 30 mg, 40 mg, 60 mg, 90 mg

Sampling Schedule	Rich Sampling	Typically as follows but could vary slightly by studies: predose, 0.25, 0.5, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 8, 12, 24, 36, 48, 72, 96, 120, 144, 168, 192, 216, 288, 360, 432, and 504 h postdose

Time-varying	CYP3A inhibitors, CYP3A inducers, antacids, H2 antagonists, PPI
L2444: Final Model	Summary	Acceptability
[FDA's comments]
Software and Version	NONMEM version 7.4.4	The structure and model fit of the quizartinib PPK model and the AC886 PPK model are adequate for characterizing the PK profiles of quizartinib and AC886.
Model Structure	
Quizartinib: a three-compartment model with sequential zero- and first-order absorption and a first-order elimination from the central compartment. Quizartinib PK varied during the different treatment phases in newly diagnosed AML subjects which was captured by including a phase effect on the relative bioavailability (F_{rel}) in the final model.
AC886: a two-compartment model with first-order elimination from the central compartment and first-order formation. The final model included a phase effect on the fraction metabolized from quizartinib (fMET) to correct for the phase effect of quizartinib (F_{rel}).

Uncertainty and Variability (RSE, IIV, Shrinkage, Bootstrap)	
As shown in Tables 20 and 25, the parameters were estimated with adequate precision. The models were able to capture the general tendency of the median data as well as their variabilities across relevant subgroups of subjects. The analysis supports the use of derived individual exposure metrics for the E-R analyses.

BLQ for Parameter Accuracy	
BLQ observations were excluded from the analysis. VPCs showed that the proportion of BLQ samples was adequately described by the model for quizartinib (Figure A7-10) and for AC886 (Figure A7-23).

GOF, VPC	
Quizartinib:
Figure A7-5: Observed quizartinib concentrations versus PREDS and IPREDS for the final quizartinib model. The upper panel shows the data on a linear scale and the lower panel shows the same plot with logarithmic scales. Individual data points are indicated by dots and the points for each individual and visit are connected with a line. The points and lines are colored according to subjects with or without AML. The diagonal black line is the line of identity and the red line is a smooth.
Goodness-of-fit (GOF) plots indicate that the final PPK models adequately fit the quizartinib and AC886 PK data overall and in subgroups of subjects with newly diagnosed AML, subjects with R/R AML, and healthy volunteers (i.e., “non-AML”). The final PPK models adequately predicted variability in quizartinib and AC886 concentrations according to the VPC plots.
The range of CWRES values was relatively
L2447: wide (-5.4 to 5.1) which suggests that some individual concentrations may not be accurately characterized by the model. However, the CWRES appeared evenly distributed around zero and the models are adequate for the purpose of predicting the average PK profiles of quizartinib and AC886.

AC886 concentrations of all studies (except studies AC220-A-U105 and AC220-A-U302) are displayed versus time after last dose on a semi-logarithmic scale and stratified by studies. Study 2689-CL-0005 is also displayed versus time after first dose in the bottom panel (orange markers delimit the bins). The solid and dashed black lines represent the median, 5th and 95th percentiles of the observations, respectively. The solid red and dashed blue lines represent the median, 5th and 95th percentiles of the predictions, respectively and the corresponding red and blue areas represent the 95% confidence interval of the percentile predicted by the model.
L2476: ## Significant
## Covariates and
## Clinical Relevance
L2477: No clinically relevant differences in quizartinib or AC886 exposure are expected according to age, weight, sex, race, weak or moderate CYP3A4 inhibitors, mild or moderate renal impairment, and mild or moderate hepatic impairment. The effect of severe renal impairment and severe hepatic impairment on quizartinib and AC886 PK has not been studied.
L2478: Figure 43: Forest plot of covariate effects for quizartinib PK parameters. The dots represent the median of the relative difference from the typical subject and the whiskers represent the 95% CI for AUCss, Css,max and Css,min, colored by the covariate. The numbers are median [95% CI] of the relative difference from the typical subject (male R/R subject, WT=75 kg, AGE= 47 years, no CYP3A co-administration and not Black or African American). CI: confidence interval; AUCss: steady-state AUC in the 24-hour dosing interval; Css,max: maximum concentration at steady-state; Css,min: minimum concentration at steady-state.
L2479: Exposure is expected to be significantly higher following the proposed 53 mg QD dosage during maintenance compared to the proposed 35.4 mg dosage in induction and consolidation, which is due to lower elimination CL and higher daily dose in maintenance treatment.
L2480: Figure 44: Forest plot of covariate effects for AC886 PK parameters. The dots represent the median of the relative difference from the typical subject and the whiskers represent the 95% CI for AUCss, Css,max and Css,min, colored by the covariate. The numbers are median [95% CI] of the relative difference from the typical subject (male R/R subject, WT=75 kg, AGE= 47 years, albumin = 4.1 g/dL, no CYP3A co-administration, not Black or African American and not Asian). CI: confidence interval; AUCss: steady-state AUC in the 24-hour dosing interval; Css,max: maximum concentration at steady-state; Css,min: minimum concentration at steady-state.
L2481: Figure 45: Forest plot of covariate effects for the sum of quizartinib and AC886 PK parameters. The dots represent the median of the relative difference from the typical subject and the whiskers represent the 95% CI for the sum of quizartinib and AC886 AUCss, Css,max and Css,min, colored by the covariate. The numbers are median [95% CI] of the relative difference from the typical subject (male R/R subject, WT=75 kg, AGE= 47 years, albumin = 4.1 g/dL, no CYP3A co-administration, not Black or African American and not Asian). CI: confidence interval; AUCss: steady-state AUC in the 24-hour dosing interval; Css,max: maximum concentration at steady-state; Css,min: minimum concentration at steady-state.
L2482: See **Table 114** and **Table 115** for a summary of quizartinib and AC886 exposure, respectively, according to treatment phase in Study AC220-A-U302. The acceptability of the proposed maintenance dosage is assessed further in Section 19.4.3.2.

L2485: Median and 95% CI exposure values according to covariate effects are displayed in forest plots in Figure 32 and Figure 33, which correspond to the exposure ratios presented in the Applicant's Figure 43 and Figure 44 in the PPK Assessment Summary, respectively.

The number of Chinese or Taiwanese or Hongkongese subjects was adapted accordingly.
L2505: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2506: The final quizartinib and AC886 population PK models are acceptable for the purpose of characterizing the average PK profile and predicting exposure in subjects with newly diagnosed AML for use in exposure-response (E-R) analyses.
L2507: The population PK dataset contained quizartinib and AC886 concentration data from a total of 931 subjects, which included 273 healthy volunteers (29%), 293 subjects with newly-diagnosed AML (31%), and 365 subjects with R/R AML (39%). Continuous covariates are summarized in non-AML subjects and for subjects with AML in the Applicant's Table 8 and Table 9 above, respectively. Categorical covariates are summarized for non-AML subjects and for subjects with AML above in the Applicant's Table 11 and Table 12 above, respectively.
L2508: The Reviewer was able to verify the Applicant's final quizartinib and AC886 PPK models without significant discrepancies in final parameter estimates. The Applicant's Table 20 and Table 25 in the PPK Assessment Summary above report the standard deviations for inter-individual variability (IIV) and residual unexplained variability (RUV) instead of the coefficient of variation (CV%). Additionally, relative standard error (RSE) percentages in the Applicant's Table 20 and Table 25 were calculated incorrectly. The summary of the Reviewer's final parameter estimates, including corrected statistics for IIV and RUV, are presented in **Table 112** for the final quizartinib model and in **Table 113** for the final AC886 model.

L2529: Source: Reviewer's run of Applicant's final AC886 model
L2530: The final AC886 model included typical values of quizartinib PK parameters that were fixed to the final estimates from the final quizartinib model. Individual values of IIV for quizartinib parameters were set to the individual posthoc IIV from the quizartinib PPK model. However, the AC886 PPK model structure did not include quizartinib T_{LAG} or quizartinib relative bioavailability (F1). Although the AC886 model estimated the fraction of quizartinib that was metabolized into AC886 (f_{MET}) and included covariate effects of treatment phase on f_{MET}, the AC886 model did not incorporate the effects of moderate and strong CYP3A inhibitors on F1 which were included in the final quizartinib model. Therefore, the AC886 model assumed that subjects receiving concomitant CYP3A inhibitors would have the same proportion of quizartinib metabolized into AC886 compared with subjects without concomitant CYP3A inhibitors.
L2531: The quizartinib and AC886 goodness of fit plots (Figures A7-5, A7-6, A7-7 for quizartinib and Figures A7-19, A7-20, and A7-21 for AC886 in the Applicant's PPK Assessment Summary) indicate adequate model fit. Although the relatively even distribution of CWRES around zero in both models indicates relatively small bias, some observed quizartinib and AC886 observed concentrations had relatively high absolute values of CWRES. The range of CWRES was -5.4 to 5.1, and no significant associations between any covariates and CWRES were identified. The final PPK models are adequate for characterizing average quizartinib and AC886 concentrations but may not accurately characterize individual concentrations.
L2532: ## **Covariate Effects**
L2533: The predicted impacts of covariates on exposure are displayed in the Applicant's Figure 43 and Figure 44 in the PPK Assessment Summary for quizartinib and AC886, respectively. **Figure 31** and **Figure 32** display the median exposure values corresponding to the Applicant's Figure 43 and Figure 44, respectively. Of note, the Applicant's forest plot of the sum of quizartinib and AC886 exposure (Figure 45 in the PPK Assessment Summary) was generated by adding quizartinib and AC886 exposure together without accounting for differences in molecular weight, which may result in errors in relative exposure difference according to covariates.

L2545: ## Body Weight
L2546: In the final quizartinib PPK model, higher body weight was associated with higher clearance and higher volume with allometric scaling (see **Table 112**). The median quizartinib AUC_{ss}, C_{max,ss}, and C_{min,ss} are 22 to 23% higher in a typical patient weighing 65 kg compared to a typical patient weighing 85 kg, as shown in **Figure 31**.
L2547: The observed OS appeared better in the quizartinib arm compared to the placebo arm across all weight quartiles, as displayed in **Figure 33**. The difference in exposure according to body weight is not expected to have clinically relevant impact on clinical outcomes, and no dose adjustments based on body weight are warranted based on current data.
L2548: **Figure 33. Study AC220-A-U302 Overall Survival versus Treatment Arm Across Baseline Body Weight Quartiles**
L2549: Weight quartile 1 = 36.8 to 61.6 kg (n=67 quizartinib; n=65 placebo). Weight quartile 2 = 61.8 to 70.2 kg (n=68 quizartinib; n=64 placebo). Weight quartile 3 = 70.3 to 82 kg (n=66 quizartinib; n=66 placebo). Weight quartile 4 = 82.1 to 151.6 kg (n=58 quizartinib; n=73 placebo). Q = quartile.
L2550: Source: Reviewer's analysis of Applicant's exposure-response efficacy dataset
L2551: ## Renal Impairment
L2552: After accounting for body weight, CL_{cr} was not associated with any clinically significant
L2553: differences in quizartinib or AC886 exposure. The PPK analysis supports the recommendation that no dose adjustment is needed in patients with mild to moderate renal impairment. The effect of severe renal impairment (CL_{cr} &lt; 30 \text{ mL/min}) on quizartinib pharmacokinetics has not been studied.
L2554: ## *Hepatic Impairment*
L2555: In the PPK dataset, 139/931 (15%) of subjects had baseline mild hepatic dysfunction and 16/931 (1.7%) subjects had baseline moderate hepatic dysfunction according to NCI-ODWG classification. Quizartinib exposure did not differ significantly according to hepatic dysfunction category. In Study AC220-A-U302, subjects with moderate hepatic dysfunction had 25% lower AC886 AUC_{ss} following planned dosing of 35.4 mg quizartinib daily during induction compared to subjects with normal hepatic function. The difference in AC886 exposure is not expected to be clinically significant.
L2556: The PPK analysis of hepatic function is consistent with the dedicated hepatic impairment studies AC220-016 and AC220-A-U105, which are described in Section 6.3.2.3. No dose adjustment is needed in patients with mild or moderate hepatic impairment. The pharmacokinetics of quizartinib have not been studied in patients with severe hepatic impairment.
L2557: ## *Race and Ethnicity*
L2558: In the PPK analysis, PK parameters did not significantly differ according to Caucasian race category or Asian race category (Caucasian [n=603] compared to not Caucasian [n=328]; Asian [n=169] compared to not Asian [n=762]). However, the PPK model identified statistically significant impacts of Black or African American race (Black or African American [n=79] compared to not Black or African American [n=852]) on quizartinib and AC886 clearance. Black or African American subjects had 35% higher median quizartinib AUC_{ss} (95% CI = 9 to 83% higher AUC_{ss}), 30% higher median quizartinib C_{max,ss} (95% CI = 7 to 70% higher C_{max,ss}), 33% lower median AC886 AUC_{ss} (95% CI = 23 to 40% lower AUC_{ss}), and 33% lower median AC886 C_{max,ss} (95% CI = 23 to 42% lower C_{max,ss}) compared to subjects who did not identify as Black or African American (see **Figure 31** and **Figure 32**).
L2559: Study AC220-A-U302 only included 2 Black or African American subjects in the quizartinib arm and so the subgroup analysis could not adequately evaluate whether identifying as Black or African American race was associated with differences in clinical safety due to potentially higher exposure. However, E-R analyses did not find any E-R safety associations where 30 to 35% higher quizartinib exposure would result in higher risk of TEAEs (see Section 19.4.3.4). Therefore, Black and African American patients with previously untreated AML are not expected to have clinically significant differences in quizartinib exposure compared to patients
L2560: of other races.
L2561: Regarding ethnicity, the PPK analysis did not identify any differences in PK parameters in Asian patients according to Japanese, Korean, Chinese, Taiwanese, or Hongkongese subgroups. Potential PK parameter differences in subjects with Hispanic/Latino ethnicity were not evaluated due to lack of Hispanic/Latino ethnicity data.
L2562: ## *Treatment Phase*
L2563: The model fit was improved when a Study AC220-A-U302 treatment phase effect was included. The Applicant included a treatment phase effect on F1 in the final quizartinib model, which resulted in a statistically significant improvement in objective function value (OFV) compared to a treatment phase effect on CL or no treatment phase effect at all.
L2564: The increase in quizartinib dose-normalized concentrations from induction to consolidation and then from consolidation to continuation may be due to changes in disease characteristics over the course of treatment. Although the PPK model adequately fits the current data, the apparent effect of treatment phase on F1 is likely explained by a correlated mechanism that is more physiologically plausible than an impact on F1. For instance, time-dependent clearance or a time-varying disease characteristic effect on quizartinib clearance may characterize PK differences more accurately than the model's treatment phase effect on F1. The model-predicted F1 of quizartinib should not be used in place of absolute bioavailability determined from clinical bioavailability data.
L2565: Median exposure following 35.4 mg once daily is 1.4-fold higher during consolidation compared to induction (AUC_{ss} = 4290 versus 3086 ng\cdothr/mL). C_{max,ss} is strongly impacted by F1 even though the treatment phase effect on F1 is physiologically unlikely, and so the PPK model may over-predict the impact of treatment phase on C_{max,ss}.
L2566: Quizartinib and AC886 exposure is higher during maintenance treatment compared to induction or consolidation due to lower clearance during maintenance as well as the higher proposed dose during maintenance. Median AUC_{ss} is 3.7-fold higher following the proposed 53 mg dose during continuation versus the proposed 35.4 mg dose during induction (AUC_{ss} = 11286 versus 3086 ng\cdothr /mL).
L2567: Based on E-R analysis of safety (Section 19.4.3.4) and E-R analysis of efficacy according to maintenance exposure (Section 19.4.3.2), the proposed quizartinib maintenance dose of 53 mg daily may result in worse QTcF prolongation and other safety events without improving survival compared to lower quizartinib maintenance dosages. The PMR in Section 6.2.2.3 addresses these concerns with the quizartinib maintenance dose and exposure.
L2568: ## *CYP3A Inhibitor Coadministration*
L2569: The PPK analysis supports the conclusion that no dosage adjustment is needed for concomitant weak or moderate CYP3A inhibitor coadministration. The PPK analysis also supports the proposed dosage reductions in patients with concomitant use of CYP3A inhibitors (see Section 6.3.2.4).
L2570: The quizartinib PPK model predicted 12% higher median quizartinib exposure (95% CI = 9 to 15%) with coadministration of a moderate CYP3A inhibitor (see **Figure 31**). This difference in quizartinib exposure is consistent with the results of Study AC220-015, which found that moderate CYP3A inhibitor coadministration resulted in 11% higher quizartinib C_{max} and 20% higher quizartinib AUC in healthy subjects. The difference in quizartinib exposure following coadministration with a moderate CYP3A inhibitor is \le 20\% and is not expected to be clinically relevant.
L2571: The quizartinib PPK model predicted that coadministration of a strong CYP3A inhibitor will result in 74% higher (95% CI = 67 to 80% higher) median quizartinib C_{max,ss} and 83% higher (95% CI = 75 to 90% higher) median quizartinib AUC_{ss}. The predicted difference in AUC is consistent with the results of Study AC220-015, which found that strong CYP3A inhibitor coadministration resulted in 17% higher quizartinib C_{max} and 94% higher quizartinib AUC in healthy subjects. The PPK model may overpredict the impact on C_{max} because the model included strong CYP3A inhibitor administration impacts on both CL and F1. Although this may have resulted in better model fit, there is no clear physiologic mechanism explaining why CYP inhibitor administration would impact quizartinib F1. However, the model-predicted impact on AUC supports the degree of exposure difference observed in Study AC220-015.
L2572: ## *CYP3A Inducer Coadministration*
L2573: Only 36/931 (3.9%) subjects with PPK data had any recorded coadministration of a weak CYP3A inducer, and 1/931 (0.1%) subject had recorded coadministration of a moderate or strong CYP3A inducer. PPK data is inadequate to assess the impact of weak, moderate, or strong CYP3A inducer coadministration on quizartinib or AC886 PK. Due to the potential for concomitant CYP3A inducer administration to decrease quizartinib exposure and impact effectiveness, the recommendation to avoid coadministration of quizartinib with moderate or strong CYP3A inducers is reasonable.
L2574: ## ***Predicted Exposure***
L2575: Study AC220-A-U302 quizartinib (**Table 114**) and AC886 (**Table 115**) exposures following planned dosage were calculated from individual predicted PK parameters for each treatment phase. Study AC220-A-U302 elimination half-lives and accumulation ratios during maintenance
L2576: treatment are summarized in Table 116.

L2608: ## 19.4.3. Exposure-Response Analysis
L2609: ## 19.4.3.1. E-R Efficacy Executive Summary
L2610: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2611: E-R analysis identified that improvement in OS may be associated with treatment arm (quizartinib versus placebo) or with higher quizartinib induction exposure (AUCss) following planned dosing of 35.4 mg daily. The data do not adequately support the conclusion that there is an E-R association with OS due to the high level of uncertainty. No E-R associations were identified between quizartinib induction exposure and EFS or CR rate.
L2612: The Reviewer also assessed OS and EFS according to maintenance exposure in subjects who received at least one maintenance dose. Maintenance exposure was not associated with any clear differences in OS or EFS. Due to confounding factors such as a potential carryover effect from quizartinib induction and consolidation as well as the QTcF-dependent maintenance dosage in the protocol, E-R analysis cannot support the conclusion that quizartinib maintenance
L2613: treatment has a clinical benefit.
L2614: Importantly, E-R analysis indicates that the proposed 53 mg daily maintenance dosage may expose patients to unnecessary toxicity such as QT prolongation and other safety events without an improvement in survival compared to lower maintenance doses. The need for additional data to determine an appropriate quizartinib maintenance dose is addressed by the PMR in Section 6.2.2.3.
L2615: ## 19.4.3.2. E-R Efficacy Assessment Summary
L2616: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2617: General Information

Endpoint	Primary: OS
Secondary: EFS (based on the definition of ITF as failure to achieve CR within 42 days from the start of the last cycle of induction chemotherapy), remission rates, and remission rates with MRD negativity

Overall Clinical Relevance for E-R	
A modest but statistically significant E-R relationship in OS, where higher quizartinib exposure was associated with longer OS, provided support for dose escalation to 60 mg in continuation treatment.
The data do not adequately support the conclusion that higher quizartinib exposure during induction is associated with better OS. The E-R analyses do not provide evidence supporting the proposed maintenance dosage of 53 mg daily. The lack of association between maintenance exposure and OS suggests that the proposed 53 mg daily maintenance dosage could expose patients to unnecessary toxicity without clinical

L2647: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2648: E-R analysis was conducted for OS, EFS, and CR rate using induction exposure (AUC_{ss} following planned dosage of 35.4 mg daily during induction). A separate E-R analysis was conducted in subjects with at least one maintenance dose using maintenance exposure from actual dosing records
L2649: ## **E-R Efficacy Analysis using Induction Steady State Exposure**
L2650: The E-R analysis of induction exposure contained data from 259 subjects with newly diagnosed FLT3-ITD(+) AML who received at least one dose of quizartinib and 268 subjects with newly diagnosed FLT3-ITD(+) AML who received at least one dose of placebo in Study AC220-A-U302.
L2651: **Table 118** summarizes the individual predicted exposures for subjects with E-R efficacy data who received quizartinib.

L2656: Source: Reviewer's analysis
L2657: Multivariate E-R modeling did not identify any clear associations between EFS or CR rate and quizartinib AUCss following planned dosage of 35.4 mg once daily.
L2658: Observed OS according to quizartinib exposure quartile is displayed in Figure 34. The lowest quizartinib AUCss quartile did not appear to have significantly different OS compared to placebo. Quizartinib quartiles Q2, Q3, and Q4 generally had better OS than placebo or Q1. However, the 95% CIs for all quartiles overlap with each other and there is no clear association between AUCss and OS.
L2659: **Figure 34. Kaplan Meier Plot of Overall Survival versus Quizartinib Induction AUCss Quartile**
L2660: Individual predicted exposure was determined following planned dosage of 35.4 mg daily during induction treatment in subjects with newly diagnosed FLT3-ITD(+) AML and assuming no CYP3A inhibitor coadministration. AML = acute myeloid leukemia; AUCss= steady state area under the concentration-versus-time curve during one dosing interval; Q = quartile.
Source: Reviewer's analysis
L2661: Several confounding factors may impact OS, such as age or baseline disease characteristics, and so multivariate E-R modeling was performed to account for potential confounding. The final OS model used a time-varying hazard function according to a Gompertz distribution, and both lower age and increased induction AUCss were associated with better OS. The final model parameter estimates from the Reviewer's run of the final model are summarized in **Table 119**. The value of the estimated AUCss slope is slightly different than the value in the Applicant's Table 20 in the E-R Efficacy Assessment Summary above due to discovery of a calculation error; however, conclusions from the model did not differ.
L2662: **Table 119. Applicant's Final Overall Survival Model Parameter Estimates**
L2663: Estimate	RSE	95% CI
Baseline Hazard (1/year)	0.669	9.1%	0.549 to 0.789
Gompertz shape parameter (1/year)	-0.619	11.1%	-0.753 to -0.485
L2664: Estimate	RSE	95% CI
Age (year) effect	0.0288	17.2%	0.019 to 0.038

L2665: Individual predicted exposure was determined following planned dosage of 35.4 mg daily during induction treatment in subjects with newly diagnosed FLT3-ITD(+) AML and assuming no CYP3A inhibitor coadministration. AML = acute myeloid leukemia; AUCss= steady state area under the concentration-versus-time curve during one dosing interval; CI = confidence interval; RSE = relative standard error.
L2666: Source: Table 20 in Applicant's Exposure-Response Report and Reviewer's analysis of Applicant's run208 model file
L2667: The VPC plots in the Applicant's Figure 23 and Figure 24 in the E-R Efficacy Assessment Summary display the variability in OS according to the Applicant's final model. However, VPC plots do not display uncertainty of model parameters or predictions, which is a critical consideration in model selection. Although the Applicant's final OS model included a statistically significant effect of quizartinib exposure on OS, reviewer analysis found that the exposure effect could be replaced by a treatment arm effect (i.e., quizartinib versus placebo) without a significant difference in model fit (OFV 4467.8 versus 4471.4, difference of 3.6 points). Therefore, it is uncertain whether the apparent effect on overall survival is truly due to quizartinib AUCss or due to treatment cohort (i.e., quizartinib or placebo). The use of exposure following planned dosing rather than actual dosing records also contributed to the uncertainty of an exposure effect on OS. The data do not adequately support the conclusion that higher quizartinib exposure is associated with better OS.
L2668: ## **Maintenance E-R Efficacy in Subjects who Received at least one Maintenance Dose**
L2669: The maintenance E-R analysis contained data for 116 subjects who received at least one maintenance dose of quizartinib and 92 subjects who received at least one maintenance dose of placebo in Study AC220-A-U302.
L2670: Subjects receiving quizartinib were less likely to receive a maximum individual dose of 53 mg, which is the full proposed once daily maintenance dose, compared to placebo. Subjects who received quizartinib had significantly higher rates of dose modifications during the maintenance phase than subjects who received placebo. The maximum individual maintenance dose and rates of dose modifications are summarized in **Table 120** and **Table 121**, respectively.

L2696: In subjects with HSCT, observed OS in the placebo arm also overlapped greatly with the quizartinib arm which suggests that quizartinib maintenance treatment may not provide a survival benefit in subjects with HSCT. In subjects without HSCT, all quartiles of maintenance C_{avg,TTE} appeared to have generally better OS compared to placebo although this finding is
L2697: uncertain due to overlapping confidence intervals between treatment arms. Additionally, all subjects who received quizartinib maintenance also received quizartinib induction and consolidation, and therefore any apparent survival benefit in subjects who received quizartinib maintenance may be due to a carryover effect from quizartinib induction and consolidation.

L2711: Because the potential impact of quizartinib C_{avg,TTE} on survival may be confounded by other patient characteristics, the Reviewer conducted multivariate Cox proportional hazard modeling for the primary efficacy endpoint of OS in subjects with at least one maintenance dose. Actual maintenance dosage records were used to calculate maintenance C_{avg,TTE}.
L2712: Potential effects from the following covariates were investigated: *treatment arm, quizartinib maintenance C_{avg,TTE}, AC886 maintenance C_{avg,TTE}, quizartinib induction AUC_{ss} following nominal dosing of 35.4 mg QD, baseline body weight, baseline ECOG score, sex, age, race, maintenance dose escalation status, AML cytogenetic risk score (favorable versus intermediate versus unfavorable), NPM1 mutation status, baseline white blood cell count (&lt;40 \times 10^9/L versus \ge 40 \times 10^9/L), HSCT status, choice of anthracycline during induction, and region.*
L2713: The model with the best fit, presented in **Table 123**, included effects from age and HSCT. Lower age and HSCT were associated with improved OS.

L2727: Evaluation of effectiveness associated with the maintenance dosage is complicated by several issues. Due to the study design of Study AC220-A-U302 and lack of re-randomization prior to maintenance treatment, the effects of quizartinib exposure and treatment during induction and consolidation phases could not be isolated from the effect of quizartinib exposure and treatment during maintenance. Subjects with higher maintenance C_{\text{avg,TTE}} tended to have higher induction exposure as well. It is not certain how survival after initiation of maintenance treatment is impacted by quizartinib maintenance independent of the impact of quizartinib induction and consolidation. Additionally, QTcF and related patient characteristics may confound the analysis because of the QTcF-dependent dose escalation during maintenance treatment.
L2728: After accounting for age and HSCT, no clear association was identified between OS and maintenance exposure or maintenance treatment (quizartinib versus placebo). The E-R
L2729: maintenance analysis did not find evidence that quizartinib maintenance treatment has a clinical benefit. Clinical analyses determined that quizartinib maintenance was beneficial (see Section 8), but the E-R maintenance analysis did not find evidence that higher maintenance exposure is associated with improved effectiveness. Given the significantly higher exposure in maintenance treatment compared to induction or consolidation (refer to Section 19.4.1.2) and the potential for higher exposures to result in worse QTcF prolongation (refer to Section 19.4.3.4) and worse tolerability, the proposed quizartinib maintenance dosage of 53 mg QD may expose patients to toxicity without providing a survival benefit compared to lower maintenance dosages. Evaluation of lower maintenance dosages is warranted in order to evaluate this significant safety concern. The PMR in Section 6.2.2.3 addresses the need for additional data to adequately evaluate maintenance quizartinib dosage.
L2730: ## 19.4.3.3. E-R Safety Executive Summary
L2731: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2732: Worse QTcF prolongation was associated with higher quizartinib concentrations. Individual observed QTcF prolongations varied widely in subjects receiving quizartinib, and QTcF measurements over +100 ms were observed in Study AC220-A-U302. The median QTcF prolongation for C_{max,ss} in the typical subject is +18.4 ms following 26.5 mg daily maintenance treatment and +24.1 ms following 53 mg daily maintenance treatment.
L2733: Higher quizartinib induction exposure may result in higher incidence of TEAE leading to dose interruption. Although E-R safety analysis was limited by use of exposure calculated with planned dosing rather than actual dosage records, no E-R associations were identified with infection, TEAE leading to dose reduction, TEAE leading to drug withdrawn, or Grade \ge 3 cytopenia incidence from the lab dataset.

L2751: ## **QTcF Prolongation**
L2752: The E-R analysis of QTcF prolongation included data from 531 subjects with newly-diagnosed AML in Study AC220-A-U302, which included 256 subjects with at least one pair of time-matched QTcF and PK measurements after first treatment dose.
L2753: The change in QTcF was characterized by an Emax model which included effects from clock time, age, hypokalemia, and individual predicted induction AUCss following planned dosage of 35.4 mg once daily as described in the Applicant's Table 28 in the E-R Safety Assessment Summary above and with 95% confidence intervals in **Table 126**. The model fit was adequate for the purpose of predicting median change in QTcF according to quizartinib concentration.

L2761: Higher quizartinib concentration was associated with greater change in QTcF. The median predicted change in QTcF according to quizartinib concentration is compared to observed data in the Applicant's Figure 31 in the E-R Safety Assessment Summary, which shows that variability in QTcF prolongation with quizartinib treatment is relatively wide. QTcF prolongations over 100 ms were observed in subjects receiving quizartinib. Both the predicted and observed QTcF in the VPC plots (Applicant's Figure 32 in the E-R Safety Assessment Summary) show the 95th percentile of QTcF exceeding 450 ms even at quizartinib concentrations below 100 ng/mL.
L2762: The model did not identify any significant associations between time-varying concomitant QT prolonging drugs and QTcF prolongation. The model also did not identify any association between time-varying concomitant beta blocker use and QTcF prolongation, but the lack of apparent association may be due to the small number of subjects with any recorded beta blocker use (49 out of 531 subjects total).
L2763: Quizartinib exposure is expected to be higher during maintenance treatment compared to induction or consolidation (see Section 19.4.1.2). Therefore, exposure during maintenance treatment is likely to result in the highest QTcF prolongation. **Table 127** summarizes the median model-predicted QTcF prolongation at the C_{\text{max,ss}} of a typical Study AC220-A-U302 patient (i.e., 70.3 kg, 56 year old male) following the proposed maintenance dosages of 26.5 mg or 53 mg once daily.
L2764: The C_{\text{max,ss}} in the typical patient (293 ng/mL following nominal maintenance dosing of 26.5 mg QD; 586 ng/mL following nominal maintenance dosing of 53 mg QD) was slightly higher than the median C_{\text{max,ss}} of the Study AC220-A-U302 population with at least one maintenance dose of quizartinib (n=115) (270 ng/mL following nominal maintenance dosing of 26.5 mg QD; 541 ng/mL following nominal maintenance dosing of 53 mg QD). The predicted C_{\text{max,ss}} in the typical subject during maintenance treatment adequately represents average exposure in Study AC220-A-U302.

L2771: ## *Incidence of Adverse Events*
L2772: The E-R safety analysis of TEAE incidence included data from 258 subjects with newly-diagnosed AML who received quizartinib and 267 subjects with newly-diagnosed AML who received placebo from Study AC220-A-U302
L2773: As summarized in the Applicant's Table 31 in the E-R Safety Assessment Summary, the Applicant's E-R analysis did not identify any associations between quizartinib AUCss following planned dosage of 35.4 mg once daily during induction and incidence of serious TEAE, TEAE leading to dose reduction or interruption, TEAE leading to discontinuation, infection, hemorrhage, hepatotoxicity, or myelosuppression in the adverse event dataset ("adae.xpt"). In the Applicant's Table 31, the term myelosuppression refers to one or more of the following adverse events in the adverse event dataset (adae.xpt): *febrile neutropenia, anemia, thrombocytopenia, neutropenia, leukopenia, platelet count decreased, white blood cell count decreased, neutrophil count decreased, pancytopenia, lymphocyte count decreased, hemoglobin decreased, hematocrit decreased, lymphopenia, or red blood cell count decreased.*
L2774: **Table 128** summarizes the Reviewer's additional E-R safety analysis of select TEAEs of interest across quizartinib induction AUCss quartiles. Higher induction AUCss was associated with a higher incidence of TEAE leading to drug interruption. No other TEAEs in **Table 128** appeared to have higher incidence in higher quizartinib AUCss quartiles. Overall, subjects who received quizartinib had higher rates of TdP/QT prolongation, TEAE leading to drug interruption, TEAE leading to dose reduction, TEAE leading to drug discontinuation, and TEAE leading to any dose modification compared to placebo in all exposure quartiles.

L2782: Although rates of cytopenias and other TEAEs were not associated with induction quizartinib AUCss, the E-R analysis did not investigate TEAE incidence according to exposure following actual dosage records in induction or other treatment phases. E-R analysis using actual exposure instead of planned exposure may be more likely to identify safety associations, and so the current E-R analysis is relatively limited. The exposure-response relationships for the safety of quizartinib have not been fully characterized.
L2783: ## **19.4.3.5. Overall Benefit-risk Evaluation Based on E-R Analyses**
L2784: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2785: The E-R analyses support the overall favorable benefit-risk for quizartinib. The E-R analysis on OS predicted a median (95% CI) HR for quizartinib compared to the placebo of 0.790 (0.690, 0.933) at the median quizartinib exposure and thus further demonstrated survival benefit from quizartinib treatment in combination with standard chemotherapy followed by continuation monotherapy in newly diagnosed AML subjects with FLT3-ITD (+) AML. Quizartinib prolongs QTcF in a concentration-dependent manner, which was appropriately managed in Study AC220-A-U302 through dose modification, including QTcF-based dosing, correction of electrolyte abnormalities, and dose adjustments for concomitant use with strong CYP3A inhibitors.
L2786: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2787: Overall, the concentration-dependent increase in QTcF interval has been characterized and the proposed dose modifications for QTcF prolongation are appropriate. The PPK and E-R analyses support a positive benefit-risk profile for the proposed dosage of 35.4 mg once daily for induction and consolidation treatment in subjects with newly diagnosed FLT3-ITD (+) AML.
L2788: The E-R analyses identified significant known risk and unclear benefit with the proposed maintenance dosage of 53 mg once daily. In subjects who received at least one maintenance dose, quizartinib exposure during maintenance treatment was not associated with improvements in OS or EFS after accounting for the effects of age and HSCT. Higher quizartinib concentrations are associated with greater QTcF prolongation, and quizartinib treatment can increase risk of safety events. Therefore, the E-R analysis indicates that 53 mg once daily during maintenance may expose patients to unnecessary toxicity without an improvement in survival. Additional data is required to determine an appropriate maintenance dose of quizartinib, and the known risk of quizartinib maintenance is addressed by the PMR in Section 6.2.2.3. Additionally, E-R analysis did not provide supportive evidence of the effectiveness of quizartinib maintenance treatment.
L2789: ## 19.4.4. Physiology Based Pharmacokinetics (PBPK) Analyses
L2790: ## 19.4.4.1. PBPK Executive Summary
L2791: The aim of this review is to evaluate the adequacy of the Applicant's PBPK report titled "development of a PBPK model for quizartinib in Simcyp population-based simulator and evaluation of DDI risk as a perpetrator of UGT1A1" to support the intended uses. Specifically, the Applicant applied the PBPK modeling approach to assess the DDI potential of quizartinib as an inhibitor of UGT1A1 and specifically, the effects of quizartinib on the exposure of raltegravir (a UGT1A1 substrate), following single dose administration of raltegravir.
L2792: The Division of Pharmacometrics has reviewed the PBPK report, supporting modeling files and concluded the following:
L2793: - The submitted PBPK models are adequate to assess the inhibitory potential of quizartinib on the exposure of raltegravir (a UGT1A1 substrate) in the healthy volunteer.
- The effect of quizartinib on a UGT1A1 substrate (such as raltegravir) is expected to be not clinically significant.
L2794: ## 19.4.4.2. Methods
L2795: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2796: ## PBPK model structure
L2797: The PBPK analyses were performed using the population based PBPK software Simcyp® (V20 Simcyp Ltd., a Certara Company, Sheffield, United Kingdom). The PBPK models for quizartinib were developed based on in vitro and in vivo information and clinical PK data. The final input parameters and the method to obtain the values of input parameters for PBPK model of quizartinib are summarized in **Table 129**.

L2805: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2806: In the PBPK model of quizartinib, the first-order absorption model was used. The fugut value was estimated using the build-in function provided by Simcyp. The upper bound and lower bound of fu is likely between 0.0088 and 1. Permeability of quizartinib was estimated by comparing the simulated and observed DDI between quizartinib and dabigatran etexilate. Thus, the confidence in the estimated permeability value is subjected, not limited to, to the PBPK model of dabigatran etexilate and the assigned ki value of P-gp for quizartinib. Sensitivity of Qgut on the effect of quizartinib on exposure of raltegravir should be conducted.
L2807: ## **PBPK model refinement and validation**
L2808: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L2809: The PBPK model was validated against the adult PK studies following single oral dose of 30 mg, 60 mg and 90 mg Quizartinib respectively in healthy subjects (AC220-014, AC220-015, AC220-018, AC220-019 and AC220-A-U106) and multiple oral doses of 30 mg and 60 mg quizartinib QD respectively in AML patients (2689-CL-2004).
L2810: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2811: The Applicant's PBPK model were verified with the PK dataset relevant to clinical conditions. The proposed initial dosing for quizartinib is 35.4 mg QD for two weeks for patients taking standard chemotherapy. Different dosing regimens are proposed for patients at different stages (for example, patients who achieved complete remission (CR)). The proposed doses range from 26.5 mg QD to 53 mg QD. Ideally, the PBPK model should be verified with a wide range of clinical PK dataset which covered the full range of clinical dosing regimens. Given the PPK analysis concludes that quizartinib showed linear pharmacokinetics in the dose range of 26.5 mg to 79.5 mg in healthy subjects and 17.7 mg to 53 mg QD in AML patients. The Applicant's modeling development and verification approach is acceptable.
L2812: ## **Raltegravir PBPK model**
L2813: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L2814: The default compound library file for raltegravir was used. Prediction performance of SV-Raltegravir model was verified with clinically observed data following 400 mg SD and 400 mg BID raltegravir treatment, respectively. A developer's summary of development and validation of the PBPK model of raltegravir is provided by the applicant.
L2815: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2816: The applicability of raltegravir PBPK model as a UGT1A substrate was discussed in PBPK review for asciminib (NDA 215358). The Reviewer noted there is a limited validation of the DDI predictive performance of raltegravir model as it was conducted using clinical DDI data of a single inhibitor, atazanavir. Raltegravir PBPK model is considered acceptable as a UGT1A substrate for purpose of risk assessment.
L2817: ## 19.4.4.3. *Results*
L2818: ## Can the PBPK model adequately describe the PK profiles of quizartinib?
L2819: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2820: The final PBPK model for quizartinib, based on both in vitro and in vivo data was able to generate plasma concentration-time profiles and exposure levels of quizartinib after a single oral dose of quizartinib 60 mg and an intravenous dose of 14C-quizartinib 50 µg in healthy male subjects that were within 1.5-fold of observed data, respectively. The model was validated against 7 sets of PK data following a single oral dose of quizartinib (30 - 90 mg). Plasma concentration-time profiles were recovered and AUCinf and Cmax were within 2-fold of the observed data. In addition, 2 sets of PK data in AML patients (30 mg QD and 60 mg QD) were used to validate the model to simulate multiple dosing. The ability of PBPK modeling to describe the clinical PK dataset at various dosing levels is presented in Figure 39.
L2821: Figure 39. PBPK Predicted and observed PK profiles of quizartinib healthy subjects and AML patients
L2822: PBPK predicted (bold lines) and observed (circle symbols) plasma concentration-time profiles of quizartinib following single intravenous dose of 50 µg (A, Study AC220-A-U107), single oral doses of 30 mg (B-E, Studies of AC220-014, AC220-015, AC220-018 and AC220-019), 60 mg (F-H, Studies of AC220-A-U107, AC220-014 and AC220-A-U106) and 90 mg (Study AC220-014) respectively in healthy subjects and multiple (QD) oral doses of 30 mg and 60 mg respectively in AML patients (Study 2689-CL-2004). The grey lines represent the 5th and 95th percentiles and the solid black lines the mean data for the simulated population. (Source: PBPK report Figures 3-11, Figures 13-14).
L2823: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2824: The submitted PBPK model can describe the PK profiles of quizartinib and adequate to support the DDI analysis with a substrate of enzyme pathway (such as UGT1A1). The fa of quizartinib was calculated to be 0.737 by dividing the observed F (0.7147) by FG (0.992) and FH (0.977). However, to recover the lower plasma exposure of quizartinib in AML patients, the fa was modified by using the ratio of simulated and observed Cmax (1.86) on the first day of the 60 mg
L2825: QD dosing (Study 2689-CL-2004) and reduced to 0.396. To simulate the effect of quizartinib on the exposure of raltegravir, the analysis was conducted in healthy virtual population where the original f_a value of 0.737 was used. Thus, a higher plasma exposure of quizartinib at steady state compared to those observed in clinical were used to conduct PBPK DDI assessment.
L2826: ## **Is the inhibitory effect of quizartinib on Raltegravir (an UGT1A1 substrate) is expected to be clinically significant?**
L2827: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L2828: Sensitivity analysis of quizartinib K_i for UGT1A1 inhibition and f_{gut} were performed by the Applicant respectively. Briefly, plasma raltegravir PK following a single oral dose of 400 mg in the absence of quizartinib (Day 1) and on the 18th day of 20 days of dosing of quizartinib (60 mg QD) to healthy subjects were simulated using 10-fold and 20-fold reduced K_i values of quizartinib for UGT1A1 (0.078 and 0.039 \muM). The impacts of different K_i values on the simulated AUC_{inf} and C_{max} ratios of raltegravir are shown in **Table 130**.
L2829: **Table 130. Effect of K_i for UGT1A1 on the simulated GMRs for raltegravir in the absence and presence of quizartinib in healthy subjects.**
L2830: K_i (\muM)	AUCinf ratio	Cmax ratio
0.78 (in vitro K_i)	1.03 (1.02 – 1.03)	1.03 (1.02 – 1.03)
0.078 (10-fold reduction)	1.24 (1.18 – 1.27)	1.21 (1.16 – 1.24)
0.039 (20-fold reduction)	1.43 (1.32 – 1.49)	1.36 (1.27 – 1.41)
L2831: GMR: Geometric mean ratio; AUCinf: simulated AUC_{408-480h}; Range of trial geometric means are shown in parentheses (Source: PBPK report Table 19).
L2832: Next, sensitivity analysis of f_{gut} was performed to evaluate the impact on the prediction of AUCinf ratio for raltegravir by increasing f_{gut} by 10-fold. As shown in Figure 40, higher f_{gut} values predicted higher AUCinf ratios and under the worst-case scenario using 20-fold reduced K_i (0.039 \muM) and 10-fold increased f_{gut} (0.38) the predicted AUC ratio for raltegravir was approximately 1.5.
L2833: Figure 40. Effect of Ki and fugut on the predicted AUC ratio for raltegravir
L2834: Raltegravir PK following a single oral dose of 400 mg raltegravir on the 18th day of 20 days of quizartinib 60 mg QD in healthy subjects. (Source: PBPK report Figure 19)
L2835: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2836: As discussed in the **Methods** section (19.4.4.2), permeability of quizartinib was estimated by comparing the simulated and observed DDI between quizartinib and dabigatran etexilate. The estimated permeability was then used to derive the Qgut value. Thus, the reviewer conducted additional sensitivity analysis to evaluate the effect of Qgut on the predicted inhibitory effect of quizartinib on raltegravir. The results of sensitivity analysis (not presented) showed that there are minimal changes in the predicted AUC ratio for raltegravir (&lt;2% change) when different Qgut (1-20 L/hr) were used in the simulations.
L2837: Reviewer also conduct a simulation of worst-case scenario using Ki of 0.039 \muM, fugut of 1 and Qgut of 20 L/h. The predicted AUC ratio for raltegravir was approximately 1.5 (Table 131). Reviewer concluded that the effect of quizartinib on a UGT1A1 substrate (such as raltegravir) is expected to be not clinically significant or weak.

L2849: ## 19.5.2. Additional Analyses of Efficacy in the Continuation Phase
L2850: ## Applicant's Analyses
L2851: To address the potential confounding issues arising from the unbalance in the prognostic factors between two treatment arms during the Continuation Phase, the sponsor provided additional analysis based on propensity scores per FDA's request. By accounting for any differences in measured baseline characteristics, the propensity score analysis mimics what would have been achieved through randomization in a randomized clinical trial. In contrast to true randomization, it should be emphasized that the propensity score can only account for measured confounders, not for any unmeasured confounders. The variables that the Applicant started with for the propensity score analysis are listed below:
L2852: - (at study baseline) age, sex, race, geographical region, ECOG performance status, choice of anthracycline, AML cytogenetic risk score, FLT3-ITD allelic burden, NPM1 mutational status, percentage of bone marrow blasts, and WBC count at the time of diagnosis of AML.
- (at the initiation of the Continuation Phase): number of induction cycles, time from randomization to start of Continuation Phase, receipt of HSCT, best response in the Induction phase, ECOG performance status, WBC count (as a continuous variable) and choice of conditioning regimen.
L2853: The Applicant chose two sets of covariates from above by selecting any unbalanced variables between two arms (1) at study baseline or the initiation of the Continuation Phase, or (2) at the initiation of the Continuation Phase, and then applied four commonly used propensity score methods to evaluate the treatment effect in the Continuation Phase for OS and RFS: stratification, matching, inverse probability treatment weighting (IPTW) and covariate adjustment based on propensity score. The corresponding results of OS overall and by HSCT status are displayed in Table 133 based on unbalanced variables between two arms at study baseline or initiation of Continuation. HR estimations for OS in the Continuation Phase from propensity score methods range from 0.72 to 0.77, which are slightly greater than the unadjusted HR of 0.68. Among 119 patients who initiated continuation with HSCT, the
L2854: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 216993}
## {VANFLYTA; quizartinib}
L2855: estimated HR from propensity score methods range from 1.61 to 1.83 in favor of placebo. Among 89 patients who initiated continuation without HSCT, the estimated HR from propensity score methods range from 0.4 to 0.46 in favor of quizartinib. These results are supportive of the previous conclusion that the benefit of quizartinib after HSCT as continuation therapy is questionable.

L2863: ## Additional FDA Analyses
L2864: Landmark analyses are survival analyses which consider only a subset of patients who have lived at least a specified amount of time. These analyses help to address immortal time bias and allow for comparisons to patients who did not experience the post-randomization event of
L2865: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 216993}
## {VANFLYTA; quizartinib}
L2866: interest. FDA conducted a landmark analysis in which only patients who survived at least 1 year after randomization are included. The choice of 1 year as the landmark time is due to (1) most patients in Continuation Analysis Set entered Continuation within 1 year after randomization, and (2) most patients with HSCT received it within 1 year after randomization. Figure 41 shows the comparison of OS between treatment arms for the subgroup of patients who entered continuation by 1 year versus not and those who received HSCT by 1 year versus not. Figure 41(d) suggests the quizartinib treatment improves OS for patients who receive continuation therapy without HSCT; in contrast, in Figure 41(b) the placebo curve stays on top of the quizartinib curve, making it unclear if patients derive benefit from continuation after HSCT.
L2867: **Figure 41: Landmark OS for patients alive at 1 year following randomization**
L2868: Source: FDA analysis
L2869: Figure 42 and Figure 43 show these results using 6- and 9-month landmarks.
L2870: **Figure 42: Landmark OS for patients alive at 6 months following randomization**
L2871: Source: FDA analysis.
L2872: Figure 43: Landmark OS for patients alive at 9 months following randomization
L2873: Source: FDA analysis.
L2874: While the above analyses correct for immortal time bias by removing patients who did not live long enough to reach HSCT or continuation therapy, they do not account for granular differences in times to these events. To address these limitations, an expanded Cox model was implemented. The expanded model includes a time-dependent indicator for HSCT and another time-dependent indicator for initiation of continuation therapy. Results from the expanded model are presented in Table 135, where the treatment effect of quizartinib during Continuation in terms of HR is estimated to 1.67 (95% CI: 0.64, 4.33) in favor of placebo among patients who received HSCT and continuation, and 0.42 (0.20, 0.88) in favor of quizartinib among patients who did not receive HSCT and continuation. These results are similar to the unadjusted results.
L2875: Table 135: Estimation of treatment effect of quizartinib on OS in the continuation phase based on the expanded Cox model (Continuation Analysis Set)
L2876: Continuation	HSCT	HR (95% CI)
No	No	0.87 (0.66, 1.15)
No	Yes	0.68 (0.36, 1.28)
Yes	No	0.42 (0.20, 0.88)
Yes	Yes	1.67 (0.64, 4.33)
L2877: Source: FDA analysis
L2878: Similar analyses were conducted on RFS. As for the adjusted OS analyses, the adjusted analyses of RFS were similar to the unadjusted RFS analyses.
L2879: As documented above, FDA requested the Applicant to conduct their own independent analysis of the effect of quizartinib 1) in the continuation setting and 2) with emphasis on possible differential effects for HSCT vs. non-HSCT patients. The results are similar to those presented above. These analyses defined the HSCT subgroup as patients who received protocol-specified HSCT rather than any HSCT.
L2880: While the analysis above suggests benefit for patients who receive quizartinib continuation without HSCT, this analysis is speculative and makes various untestable, strong assumptions. One such assumption is that the treatment effect (i.e., HR) due to any treatment period (induction/consolidation, continuation) is constant. For instance, the above analysis estimates the effect of induction/consolidation quizartinib therapy on OS to be 0.87 (95% CI: 0.66, 1.15). Though it is possible that continued quizartinib continuation increases this treatment effect for patients who do not receive HSCT, it is also possible that this treatment effect would increase on its own over time without further treatment. Similar logic applies to patients who receive HSCT and subsequent quizartinib continuation therapy. Because QuANTUM-First was not re-randomized during the continuation phase, more rigorous comparisons cannot be made.
L2881: ## 19.5.3. Effect of MRD in Continuation
L2882: Among 124 patients in MRD- Continuation Analysis Set, 70 (56%) and 54 (44%) started the continuation therapy with and without HSCT respectively. The OS analysis results among these patients and a breakdown by the HSCT status are provided in Figure 44. There is a separation of Kaplan-Meier curves is in favor of quizartinib (HR = 0.43 [95% CI: 0.18, 1.03]), although such an effect is mostly driven by the patients without HSCT prior to continuation (HR = 0.28 [95% CI: 0.09, 0.89]). Whereas among the patients with HSCT prior to continuation, the survival curves overlap for most of the time, which suggests lack of benefit of the quizartinib treatment as continuation therapy in this subgroup.
L2883: Figure 44 Kaplan-Meier plot of OS in Continuation Analysis Set (overall and by HSCT status, MRD- only)
L2884: Source: Applicant's information response dated 9 January 2023

L2923: Figure 1 is a schematic diagram illustrating the Study Design (Screening to Treatment Period) for Study AC220-A-U302. The study population consists of subjects with FLT3-ITD (+) Newly Diagnosed AML, aged \ge 18 and \le 75 years old.
The study is divided into three main phases: Induction Phase, Consolidation Phase, and Continuation Phase, followed by a Long-term Follow-up period.
Induction Phase (up to 2 cycles):
Arm 1 (Quizartinib arm): Cytarabinea (Days 1 – 8), Anthracyclinea (Days 1 – 3), Quizartinib (QD Days 8 – 21).
Arm 2 (Placebo arm): Cytarabinea (Days 1 – 8), Anthracyclinea (Days 1 – 3), Placebo (QD Days 8 – 21).
Consolidation Phase (with chemotherapy and/or allo-HSCT):
Arm 1 (Quizartinib arm): Cytarabinea (Days 1, 3, & 5), Quizartinib (QD Days 6 – 19).
Arm 2 (Placebo arm): Cytarabinea (Days 1, 3, & 5), Placebo (QD Days 6 – 19).
Continuation Phase (up to 36):
Arm 1 (Quizartinib arm): Quizartinib (QD).
Arm 2 (Placebo arm): Placebo (QD).
Randomization: Cycle 1 Day 7 (+3 days).
L2924: Kaplan-Meier Plot of Overall Survival in Study AC220-A-U302 (ITT Analysis Set).
The plot shows Overall Survival Probability (Y-axis, 0.0 to 1.0) versus Time (Months) (X-axis, 0 to 60). The blue line represents Quizartinib (N=268) and the red line represents Placebo (N=271). The blue line consistently shows a higher survival probability than the red line.
Key statistical results displayed on the plot:
Hazard Ratio = 0.776
95% CI = (0.615, 0.979)
p-value (2-sided, stratified log-rank) = 0.0324

L2931: Strata: Placebo (red line) and Quizartinib (blue line).
The figure displays four Kaplan-Meier survival curves showing Overall Survival (OS) probability versus Time (in months) for HSCT receivers who received maintenance therapy, stratified by MRD status at HSCT.
MRD- CR at HSCT: Both Placebo and Quizartinib groups show similar survival probabilities, decreasing from 1.00 to approximately 0.65 over 48 months.
MRD+ CR at HSCT: The Quizartinib group maintains a significantly higher survival probability (around 0.85) compared to the Placebo group (around 0.55) over 48 months.
Not in CR at HSCT: Both groups show similar survival probabilities, decreasing from 1.00 to approximately 0.55 over 48 months.
Unknown MRD CR at HSCT: The Quizartinib group maintains a high survival probability (around 0.85) while the Placebo group shows a sharp drop in survival probability after an initial period (around 0.35 at 18 months).

L2947: FLT3-ITD AML
Age, \ge 18 years
Refractory AML or relapse within 6 months of first remission (\pm HSCT)
\ge 1 cycle of standard-dose anthracycline- or mitoxantrone-containing induction therapy
\ge 3\% FLT3-ITD allelic ratio
Primary endpoint
Overall survival
Secondary and exploratory endpoints
Event-free survival, CRc rate, duration of CRc, and transplant rate
Study Design Flow:
Subjects are randomized 2:1.
Quizartinib arm: Leads to HSCT, followed by Quizartinib continuation, leading to Follow-up.
Salvage chemotherapy (LoDAC, MEC, or FLAG-IDA) arm: Leads to HSCT, leading to Follow-up.
Optional treatments (applies to HSCT step):
HSCT based on local practice
Tumor control and reduction
Fitness
Donor availability

L2983: Figure 37 is a Kaplan-Meier plot showing Observed Event-Free Survival (EFS) versus Days Since First Day of Maintenance for subjects who received at least one maintenance dose, stratified by maintenance quizartinib average concentration (C_{avg}) quartile. Strata (Red dashed line): C_{avg}=Placebo
Strata (Yellow dashed line): C_{avg}=Q1
Strata (Green dashed line): C_{avg}=Q2
Strata (Blue dashed line): C_{avg}=Q3
Strata (Purple dashed line): C_{avg}=Q4
Dashed lines represent 95% confidence intervals. The plot shows that EFS generally decreases over time for all strata. The C_{avg}=Q4 stratum maintains the highest EFS rate, followed by Q3, Q2, Q1, and Placebo, although confidence intervals overlap significantly, especially for Q3 and Q4 compared to Q1 and Q2.

L2984: Kaplan-Meier plot showing Event-free Survival versus Days Since First Day of Maintenance for subjects who received at least one maintenance dose, stratified by HSCT status (No HSCT Flag vs. Yes HSCT Flag). The plot compares Placebo (red) and four maintenance quizartinib quartiles (Q1, Q2, Q3, Q4, represented by yellow, green, blue, and purple lines, respectively). Dashed lines represent 95% confidence intervals. The X-axis ranges from 0 to 1500 days, and the Y-axis ranges from 0.0 to 1.0. In the No HSCT Flag group, Q1 shows the highest survival, followed by Q2, Q3, and Q4, with Placebo showing the lowest survival. In the Yes HSCT Flag group, Q1 also shows the highest survival, followed by Q2, Q3, and Q4, with Placebo showing the lowest survival.

The observed 5th and 95th percentiles are also shown as shaded areas. The predicted QTcF generally exceeds 450 ms, especially at higher quizartinib concentrations.

L2989: Figure 40 is a line graph showing the predicted AUC ratio for raltegravir (Y-axis, ranging from 1.00 to 1.75) versus UGT1A1 Ki (X-axis, ranging from 0 to 0.8). The AUC ratio decreases as UGT1A1 Ki increases, and the lines are generally close together, indicating minimal sensitivity to changes in fugut.

L2992: OS for Patients Alive at 9 Months Following Randomization
